By direct computation, we obtain the following equation

\bar{R}_{w}K^{(t)}_{w}(z)=(n+1+t)\frac{\langle z,w\rangle}{(1-\langle z,w\rangle)^{n+2+t}}=(n+1+t)K^{(t+1)}_{w}(z)-(n+1+t)K^{(t)}_{w}(z).

So we obtain the following estimates,

|\bar{R}_{w}K^{(t)}_{w}(z)|\lesssim|w||K^{(t+1)}_{w}(z)|,\quad\big|\bar{R}_{w}K^{(t)}_{w}(z)-(n+1+t)K^{(t+1)}_{w}(z)\big|\lesssim|K^{(t)}_{w}(z)|.

(5.7)

Also by Lemma
5.8
, we get the following inequalities

|\mathcal{G}^{(t)}_{n}1(s)|\lesssim s^{-n},\quad\big|\mathcal{G}^{(t)}_{n}1(s)-\frac{1}{t+1}\big|\lesssim s^{-n}(1-s).

(5.8)

By (
5.7
) and (
5.8
), we read

\displaystyle\big|Y^{(t)}_{w}(z)\big|\lesssim

\displaystyle|w|^{-2n}|K^{(t)}_{w}(z)|,

and

\displaystyle\big|X^{(t)}_{w}(z)\big|=

\displaystyle\bigg|\bigg((t+1)\mathcal{G}^{(t)}_{n}1(|w|^{2})-1\bigg)\cdot\frac{\bar{R}_{w}K^{(t)}_{w}(z)}{n+t+1}+\bigg(\frac{\bar{R}_{w}K^{(t)}_{w}(z)}{n+t+1}-K^{(t+1)}_{w}(z)\bigg)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg|(t+1)\mathcal{G}^{(t)}_{n}1(|w|^{2})-1\bigg|\cdot\bigg|\frac{\bar{R}_{w}K^{(t)}_{w}(z)}{n+t+1}\bigg|+\bigg|\frac{\bar{R}_{w}K^{(t)}_{w}(z)}{n+t+1}-K^{(t+1)}_{w}(z)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|w|^{-2n}(1-|w|^{2})\cdot|w|\big|K^{(t+1)}_{w}(z)\big|+\big|K^{(t)}_{w}(z)\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|w|^{-2n+1}\big|K^{(t)}_{w}(z)\big|.

This completes the proof of Lemma
5.16
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.1
.

Take
K_{i}=f_{i}(0)E_{0}
. Then each
K_{i}
is a rank one operator. Take
X_{w}(z)=X^{(t)}_{w}(z)
and
Y_{w}(z)=Y^{(t)}_{w}(z)
and define
\hat{A}_{i}
,
\hat{B}_{i}
,
\hat{C}_{i}
as in Proposition
5.15
. Then clearly they satisfy

A_{i}=\hat{A}_{i},\quad B_{i}=\hat{B}_{i},\quad C_{i}=\hat{C}_{i},\quad i=1,\ldots,2n.

By Lemma
5.16
,
X_{w}(z)
and
Y_{w}(z)
satisfy Hypotheses B at
t
. Thus by Proposition
5.15
, we verify

\displaystyle[T^{(t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t)}_{f_{2n}}]-[T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{2n}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[C_{1},C_{2},\ldots,C_{2n}]-[A_{1},A_{2},\ldots,A_{2n}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\hat{C}_{1},\hat{C}_{2},\ldots,\hat{C}_{2n}]-[\hat{A}_{1},\hat{A}_{2},\ldots,\hat{A}_{2n}]

is a trace class operator with zero trace. This completes the proof of Lemma
5.1
.
∎

Next, we prove Lemma
5.2
.

Lemma 5.17
.

The integral kernels
X^{(-1)}_{w}(z),Y^{(-1)}_{w}(z)
and
Z^{(-1)}_{w}(z)
satisfy the following estimates.

1.

|X^{(-1)}_{w}(z)|\lesssim|w|^{-2n+1/2}|K^{(0)}_{w}(z)|
;

2.

|Y^{(-1)}_{w}(z)|\lesssim|w|^{-2n+1/2}|K^{(0)}_{w}(z)|
;

3.

|Z^{(-1)}_{w}(z)|\lesssim|w|^{-2n-1/2}|K_{w}(z)|
.

In particular,
X^{(-1)}_{w}(z),Y^{(-1)}_{w}(z)
satisfy Hypotheses B at
t=0
.

Proof.

We compute the following limit by L’Hospital’s rule,

\lim_{s\to 1^{-}}\frac{\mathcal{G}_{n}^{(0)}\phi(s)-1}{1-s}=\lim_{s\to 1^{-}}\frac{\int_{s}^{1}r^{-1}\mathrm{d}r-(1-s)}{(1-s)^{2}}=\lim_{s\to 1^{-}}\frac{-s^{-1}+1}{-2(1-s)}=\frac{1}{2}.

Also by
[
47
, Lemma 8.2]
with
a=n+\frac{1}{4}
,
\mathcal{G}_{n}^{(0)}\phi(s)\lesssim s^{-n-\frac{1}{4}}
. Therefore we obtain the estimates

\bigg|\mathcal{G}_{n}^{(0)}\phi(s)-1\bigg|\lesssim s^{-n-\frac{1}{4}}(1-s),\quad\big|\mathcal{G}_{n}^{(0)}\phi(s)\big|\lesssim s^{-n-\frac{1}{4}}.

(5.9)

Also, direct computation gives the following identities

\bar{R}_{w}K_{w}(z)=\frac{n\langle z,w\rangle}{(1-\langle z,w\rangle)^{n+1}}=nK^{(0)}_{w}(z)-nK_{w}(z),

\bar{R}^{2}_{w}K_{w}(z)=\frac{n(n+1)\langle z,w\rangle}{(1-\langle z,w\rangle)^{n+2}}-\frac{n^{2}\langle z,w\rangle}{(1-\langle z,w\rangle)}=n(n+1)K_{w}^{(1)}(z)-n(2n+1)K_{w}^{(0)}(z)+n^{2}K_{w}(z).

So we have the following estimates,

\bigg|\bar{R}_{w}^{2}K_{w}(z)-n(n+1)K_{w}^{(1)}(z)\bigg|\lesssim|K_{w}^{(0)}(z)|,

(5.10)

and

|\bar{R}_{w}^{2}K_{w}(z)|\lesssim|w||K^{(1)}_{w}(z)|,\quad|\bar{R}_{w}K_{w}(z)|\lesssim|w||K_{w}^{(0)}(z)|.

(5.11)

By (
5.9
), (
5.10
) and (
5.11
), we estimate
|X^{(-1)}_{w}(z)|,|Y^{(-1)}_{w}(z)|,|Z^{(-1)}_{w}(z)|
as follows,

\displaystyle|X^{(-1)}_{w}(z)|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n(n+1)}\bigg|\big(\mathcal{G}_{n}^{(0)}\phi(|w|^{2})-1\big)\bar{R}^{2}_{w}K_{w}(z)\bigg|+\bigg|\frac{1}{n(n+1)}\bar{R}^{2}_{w}K_{w}(z)-K_{w}^{(1)}(z)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|w|^{-2n-\frac{1}{2}}(1-|w|^{2})|w||K_{w}^{(1)}(z)|+|K_{w}^{(0)}(z)|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|w|^{-2n+\frac{1}{2}}|K_{w}^{(0)}(z)|,

\displaystyle|Y^{(-1)}_{w}(z)|\lesssim

\displaystyle|w|^{-2n-\frac{1}{2}}|w||K_{w}^{(0)}(z)|=|w|^{-2n+\frac{1}{2}}|K_{w}^{(0)}(z)|,

and

\displaystyle|Z^{(-1)}_{w}(z)|\lesssim

\displaystyle|w|^{-2n-\frac{1}{2}}|K_{w}(z)|.

This completes the proof of Lemma
5.17
.
∎

By Lemma
5.17
and Lemma
5.14
, for
f\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
, the integral formulas of
X^{(-1)}_{f}
and
Y^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}f}
define bounded operators on
L_{a,0}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})
. Denote these operators
X^{(-1,0)}_{f}
and
Y^{(-1,0)}_{\bar{R}f}
.

Lemma 5.18
.

Suppose
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. Then the integral formula of
Z^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}^{2}f}
defines a bounded operator on
L_{a,0}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})
that belongs to
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>\frac{2n}{3}
. Denote this operator to be
Z^{(-1,0)}_{\bar{R}^{2}f}
.

Proof.

Split the map as follows.

Z^{(-1,0)}_{\bar{R}^{2}f}:L_{a,0}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})\xrightarrow{E_{0,3-2\epsilon}}L_{a,3-2\epsilon}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})\xrightarrow{\hat{Z}^{(-1,0)}_{\bar{R}^{2}f}}L_{a}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n}).

Here
\hat{Z}^{(-1,0)}_{\bar{R}^{2}f}
is defined by the same integral formula as
Z^{(-1,0)}_{\bar{R}^{2}f}
, and
\epsilon>0
is any sufficiently small number. The boundedness of
\hat{Z}^{(-1,0)}_{\bar{R}^{2}f}
follows from Lemma
5.17
and Lemma
3.1
. Finally, by Lemma
3.4
,
E_{0,3-2\epsilon}\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>\frac{n}{\frac{3}{2}-\epsilon}
. Since
\epsilon>0
is arbitrary, we have
Z^{(-1,0)}_{\bar{R}^{2}f}\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>\frac{2n}{3}
. This completes the proof of Lemma
5.18
.
∎

By Lemma
5.4
, for
f\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
, the operator

T^{-1,0}_{f}=T^{(1,0)}_{f}+X^{(-1,0)}_{f}+Y^{(-1,0)}_{\bar{R}f}+Z^{(-1,0)}_{\bar{R}^{2}f}+f(0)E_{0}

is a well-defined bounded operator on
L_{a,0}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})
. It follows from Lemma
5.17
, Lemma
5.18
and Proposition
5.15
that the following holds.

Lemma 5.19
.

Suppose
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{2n}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. Then the operator on
L_{a,0}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})
,

[T_{f_{1}}^{(-1,0)},T^{(-1,0)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(-1,0)}_{f_{2n}}]-[T^{(1,0)}_{f_{1}},T^{(1,0)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(1,0)}_{f_{2n}}]

is a trace class operator of zero trace.

Lemma 5.20
.

Suppose
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
.

1.

The operators
X^{(-1)}_{f},Y^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}f}
on
H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})
belong to
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>n
.

2.

The operator
Z^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}^{2}f}
on
H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})
belongs to
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>\frac{n}{2}
.

Proof.

If
f
has compact support in
\mathbb{B}_{n}
, then
X^{(-1)}_{f},Y^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}f},Z^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}^{2}f}
belong to the trace class. Since have the decomposition
f=f_{1}+f_{2}
, where
f_{1}
has compact support in
\mathbb{B}_{n}
, and
f_{2}
has support away from the origin, we might as well assume that the support of
f
does not contain the origin. Then by Lemma
5.17
, we obtain the following bounds

|f(w)X^{(-1)}_{w}(z)|\lesssim|K_{w}^{(0)}(z)|,\quad|\bar{R}f(w)Y^{(-1)}_{w}(z)|\lesssim|K_{w}^{(0)}(z)|,\quad|\bar{R}^{2}Z^{(-1)}_{w}(z)|\lesssim|K_{w}(z)|.

Split the maps as follows.

X^{(-1)}_{f}:H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})\xrightarrow{E_{-1,2}}L_{a,1}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})\xrightarrow{\hat{X}^{(-1)}_{f}}H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n}),

Y^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}f}:H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})\xrightarrow{E_{-1,2}}L_{a,1}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})\xrightarrow{\hat{Y}^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}f}}H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n}),

Z^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}^{2}f}:H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})\xrightarrow{E_{-1,4-2\epsilon}}L_{a,3-2\epsilon}\xrightarrow{\hat{Z}^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}^{2}f}}H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n}).

Here the operators with hats are defined by the same integral formulas as the corresponding operators without hats, and
\epsilon>0
is any sufficiently small number. The boundedness of the operators with hats follow from the estimates in Lemma
5.17
and Lemma
3.3
. Finally, by Lemma
3.4
, the Schatten-class memberships of
X^{(-1)}_{f},Y^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}f}
and
Z^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}^{2}f}
follow from those of the embedding operators. This completes the proof of Lemma
5.20
.
∎

Remark 5.21
.

It follows from Lemma
5.6
and Lemma
5.20
that for any
f\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
, the operator
T^{(1,-1)}_{f}=T_{f}-X^{(-1)}_{f}-Y^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}f}-Z^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}^{2}f}-f(0)E_{0}
is a well-defined bounded operator on
H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})
.

Lemma 5.22
.

Suppose
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. Then the commutators

[X^{(-1)}_{f},T^{(1,-1)}_{g}],\quad[Y^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}f},T^{(1,-1)}_{f}]

belong to
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>\frac{2n}{3}
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
5.20
, we may assume the support of
f
is away from the origin. For any
h\in H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n}),
since
X^{(-1)}_{w}(z),Y^{(-1)}_{w}(z)
are holomorphic in
z
, it is easy to verify the following formulas,

\bigg(T^{(1,-1)}_{g}X^{(-1)}_{f}-X^{(-1)}_{gf}\bigg)h(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}\big[g(z)-g(w)\big]f(w)h(w)K^{(1)}_{z}(\xi)X^{(-1)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{1}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{1}(z),

\bigg(X^{(-1)}_{f}T^{(1,-1)}_{g}-X^{(-1)}_{gf}\bigg)h(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}f(z)\big[g(w)-g(z)\big]h(w)X^{(-1)}_{z}(\xi)K^{(1)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{1}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{1}(z),

and similarly,

\bigg(T^{(1,-1)}_{g}Y^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}f}-Y^{(-1)}_{g\bar{R}f}\bigg)h(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}\big[g(z)-g(w)\big]\bar{R}f(w)h(w)K^{(1)}_{z}(\xi)Y^{(-1)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{1}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{1}(z),

\bigg(Y^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}f}T^{(1,-1)}_{g}-Y^{(-1)}_{g\bar{R}f}\bigg)h(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}\bar{R}f(z)\big[g(w)-g(z)\big]h(w)Y^{(-1)}_{z}(\xi)K^{(1)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{1}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{1}(z).

If we denote
G_{w}(z)
to be either
f(w)X^{(-1)}_{w}(z)
or
\bar{R}f(w)Y^{(-1)}_{w}(z)
, then set

\bigg(T^{(1,-1)}_{g}X^{(-1)}_{f}-X^{(-1)}_{gf}\bigg),\text{ or }\bigg(T^{(1,-1)}_{g}Y^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}f}-Y^{(-1)}_{g\bar{R}f}\bigg)=H,

\bigg(X^{(-1)}_{f}T^{(1,-1)}_{g}-X^{(-1)}_{gf}\bigg),\text{ or }\bigg(Y^{(-1)}_{\bar{R}f}T^{(1,-1)}_{g}-Y^{(-1)}_{g\bar{R}f}\bigg)=WS.

Here

Sh(z)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\big[g(w)-g(z)\big]h(w)K^{(1)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{1}(w),\ Wh(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}h(z)G_{z}(\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{1}(z),

and

Hh(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}[g(z)-g(w)]h(w)G_{w}(z)K^{(1)}_{z}(\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{1}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{1}(z)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}h(w)H_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{1}(w),

where

H_{w}(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}[g(z)-g(w)]G_{w}(z)K^{(1)}_{z}(\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{1}(z).

By Lemma
5.17
and since we assume the support of
f
does not contain the origin, we obtain the following bound

|G_{w}(z)|\lesssim|K^{(0)}_{w}(z)|.

By Lemma
2.3
, we have the estimate

|H_{w}(\xi)|\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\frac{1}{|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{n+1/2}|1-\langle z,\xi\rangle|^{n+2}}\mathrm{d}\lambda_{1}(\xi)\lesssim\frac{1}{|1-\langle w,\xi\rangle|^{n+1/2}}.

For any
\epsilon>0
sufficiently small, split the map as follows.

H:H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})\xrightarrow{E_{-1,3-2\epsilon}}L_{a,2-2\epsilon}\xrightarrow{\hat{H}}H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n}),

WS:H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})\xrightarrow{E_{-1,3-2\epsilon}}L_{a,2-2\epsilon}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})\xrightarrow{\hat{S}}L^{2}(\lambda_{1-2\epsilon})\xrightarrow{\hat{W}}H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n}).

By the estimates above, Lemma
3.1
and Lemma
3.3
, the operators above with hats are bounded. Thus by Lemma
3.4
,
H
and
WS
belong to
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>\frac{2n}{3}
. Therefore the commutators have the same Schatten-class membership. This completes the proof of Lemma
5.22
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.2
.

By Lemma
5.19
, the operator

[T^{(-1,0)}_{f_{1}},\ldots,T^{(-1,0)}_{f_{2n}}]-[T^{(1,0)}_{f_{1}},\ldots,T^{(1,0)}_{f_{2n}}]

on
L_{a,0}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})
is a trace class operator with zero trace. On the other hand, by Lemma
5.20
, Lemma
5.22
and Proposition
5.9
, the operator

[T_{f_{1}},\ldots,T_{f_{2n}}]-[T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{1}},\ldots,T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{2n}}]

is a trace class operator on
H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})
. Clearly, we check the following equation

\bigg([T^{(-1,0)}_{f_{1}},\ldots,T^{(-1,0)}_{f_{2n}}]-[T^{(1,0)}_{f_{1}},\ldots,T^{(1,0)}_{f_{2n}}]\bigg)\bigg|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})}=[T_{f_{1}},\ldots,T_{f_{2n}}]-[T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{1}},\ldots,T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{2n}}].

Thus by Lemma
2.4
, we obtain the following equation

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\bigg([T_{f_{1}},T_{f_{2}},\ldots,T_{f_{2n}}]-[T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{1}},T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{2n}}]\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\bigg([T^{(-1,0)}_{f_{1}},T^{(-1,0)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(-1,0)}_{f_{2n}}]-[T^{(1,0)}_{f_{1}},T^{(1,0)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(1,0)}_{f_{2n}}]\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

This completes the proof of Lemma
5.2
.
∎

5.3
Proof of Proposition
5.15

Lemma 5.23
.

Suppose
t>-1
and
f
,
g
are Lipschitz functions on
\mathbb{B}_{n}
. Then

[T^{(t+1,t)}_{f},T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}]\in\mathcal{S}^{p},\quad\forall p>n.

Proof.

Denote
P^{(t+1,t)}
to be the restriction of
P^{(t+1)}
to
L^{2}(\lambda_{t})
. By Lemma
3.1
it is easy to see that
P^{(t+1,t)}
is bounded. Obviously,
P^{(t+1,t)}
satisfies the following identities.

P^{(t)}P^{(t+1,t)}=P^{(t+1,t)},\quad P^{(t+1,t)}P^{(t)}=P^{(t)},\quad\big(P^{t+1,t}\big)^{2}=P^{(t+1,t)}.

So we compute the commutator as follows.

\displaystyle[T^{(t+1,t)}_{f},T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[P^{(t+1,t)}M_{f}P^{(t)},P^{(t+1,t)}M_{g}P^{(t)}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P^{(t+1,t)}M_{f}P^{(t+1,t)}M_{g}P^{(t)}-P^{(t+1,t)}M_{g}P^{(t+1,t)}M_{f}P^{(t)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P^{(t+1,t)}M_{g}\big(I-P^{(t+1,t)}\big)M_{f}P^{(t)}-P^{(t+1,t)}M_{f}\big(I-P^{(t+1,t)}\big)M_{g}P^{(t)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P^{(t+1,t)}[M_{g},P^{(t+1,t)}][M_{f},P^{t+1,t}]P^{(t)}-P^{(t+1,t)}[M_{f},P^{(t+1,t)}][M_{g},P^{t+1,t}]P^{(t)}.

By Corollary
4.3
, we arrive at the following property,

[M_{g},P^{(t+1,t)}],\quad[M_{f},P^{(t+1,t)}]\in\mathcal{S}^{p},~\forall p>2n.

The Schatten-
p
membership of the commutator
[T^{(t+1,t)}_{f},T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}]
follows from the above property easily. This completes the proof of Lemma
5.23
.
∎

Lemma 5.24
.

Assume Hypotheses B. Then for any
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
, the commutators

[X_{f},T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}]\text{ and }[Y_{\bar{R}f},T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}]

belong to
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for some
p<n
.

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
5.14
, we may assume the support of
f
does not contain the origin. For any
h\in L_{a,t}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n}),
since
X_{w}(z),Y_{w}(z)
are holomorphic in
z
, it is easy to verify the following integral expression,

\bigg(T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}X_{f}-X_{gf}\bigg)h(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}\big[g(z)-g(w)\big]f(w)h(w)K^{(t+1)}_{z}(\xi)X_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z),

\bigg(X_{f}T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}-X_{gf}\bigg)h(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}f(z)\big[g(w)-g(z)\big]h(w)X_{z}(\xi)K^{(t+1)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z),

and similarly,

\bigg(T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}Y_{\bar{R}f}-Y_{g\bar{R}f}\bigg)h(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}\big[g(z)-g(w)\big]\bar{R}f(w)h(w)K^{(t+1)}_{z}(\xi)Y_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z),

\bigg(Y_{\bar{R}f}T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}-Y_{g\bar{R}f}\bigg)h(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}\bar{R}f(z)\big[g(w)-g(z)\big]h(w)Y_{z}(\xi)K^{(t+1)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z).

As in the proof of Lemma
5.14
, let
G_{w}(z)
be either
f(w)X_{w}(z)
or
\bar{R}f(w)Y_{w}(z)
. Write

Th(z)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}[g(z)-g(w)]h(w)G_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(w),

Sh(z)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\big[g(w)-g(z)\big]h(w)K^{(t+1)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(w),

and

Wh(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}h(z)G_{z}(\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z).

As mappings, set

T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}X_{f}-X_{gf},\text{ or }T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}Y_{\bar{R}f}-Y_{g\bar{R}f}=P^{(t+1)}T,

X_{f}T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}-X_{gf},\text{ or }Y_{\bar{R}f}T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}-Y_{g\bar{R}f}=WS.

Take
\epsilon>0
small enough. Split
P^{(t+1)}T
and
WS
as the composition of the following operators.

P^{(t+1)}T:L_{a,t}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})\xrightarrow{E_{t,2+\epsilon}}L_{a,t+2+\epsilon}^{2}\xrightarrow{T}L^{2}(\lambda_{t})\xrightarrow{P^{(t+1)}}L_{a,t}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n}),

WS:L_{a,t}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})\xrightarrow{E_{t,2+\epsilon}}L_{a,t+2+\epsilon}^{2}\xrightarrow{S}L^{2}(\lambda_{t+2})\xrightarrow{W}L^{2}(\lambda_{t}).

By Lemma
2.2
, we have the following inequalities,

|g(z)-g(w)|\lesssim|z-w|\lesssim|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{1/2}.

By Lemma
3.1
,
T,P^{(t+1)},S,W
define bounded operators between the spaces indicated above. Again, by Lemma
3.4
,
E_{t,2+\epsilon}\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
for some
p<n
. Thus altogether, the operators

\bigg(T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}X_{f}-X_{gf}\bigg),\bigg(T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}Y_{\bar{R}f}-Y_{g\bar{R}f}\bigg),\bigg(X_{f}T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}-X_{gf}\bigg),\bigg(Y_{\bar{R}f}T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}-Y_{g\bar{R}f}\bigg)

are in
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for some
p<n
. Thus so do the commutators

[T^{(t+1,t)}_{g},X_{f}]=\bigg(T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}X_{f}-X_{gf}\bigg)-\bigg(X_{f}T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}-X_{gf}\bigg),

and

[T^{(t+1,t)}_{g},Y_{\bar{R}f}]=\bigg(T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}Y_{\bar{R}f}-Y_{g\bar{R}f}\bigg)-\bigg(Y_{\bar{R}f}T^{(t+1,t)}_{g}-Y_{g\bar{R}f}\bigg).

This completes the proof of Lemma
5.24
.
∎

From Lemma
5.14
, Lemma
5.23
and Lemma
5.24
it follows that if we set

A_{i}=T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{i}},\quad B_{i}=X_{f_{i}}+Y_{\bar{R}f_{i}}+K_{i},\quad i=1,2,\ldots,2n,

then the operators
\{A_{i},B_{i}\}
satisfy Hypotheses A defined Subsection
5.1
. Thus by Proposition
5.9
, the proof of Proposition
5.15
reduces to the proof of

\mathrm{Tr}\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{2n}[A_{1},\ldots,A_{k-1},B_{k},A_{k+1},\ldots,A_{2n}]\bigg)=0.

Each
B_{k}
splits into the sum of
X_{f_{k}}
,
Y_{\bar{R}f_{k}}
and
K_{k}
. The part with
K_{k}
can be handled by Corollary
5.12
as
K_{k}
is assumed to belong to
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for some
p<n
. Thus it remains to prove

\mathrm{Tr}\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{2n}[A_{1},\ldots,A_{k-1},X_{f_{k}}+Y_{\bar{R}f_{k}},A_{k+1},\ldots,A_{2n}]\bigg)=0.

(5.12)

As explained in Remark
5.13
, it is hard to handle this trace at operator-theoretic level. Instead, we need to treat them as integral operators. Let us explain the idea of the proof. The operator in (
5.12
) is a sum of compositions of
2n
integral operators. By
[
47
, Lemma 2.5]
and the definitions of
A_{i},X_{f_{k}},Y_{\bar{R}f_{k}}
, we can write the trace in (
5.12
) into a
(2n+1)
-fold integral of the form

\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\bigg[\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2n}}K^{(t)}_{\xi}(z_{1})G(z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{2n},\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{1})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{2n})\bigg]\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(\xi),

where
G(z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{2n},\xi)
is holomorphic in
\xi
. If the
(2n+1)
-fold integral converges absolutely, then we can apply Fubini’s Theorem and get that the above integral is equal to

\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2n}}G(z_{1},z_{2},\ldots,z_{2n},\xi)\big|_{\xi=z_{1}}\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{1})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{2n}).

The anti-symmetrization will then tell us that the above equals zero as
\xi=z_{1}
. Thus the proof of Proposition
5.15
reduces to reorganize the parts in (
5.12
) so that each part converges absolutely.

The following lemma helps us recognizing absolutely integrable terms in a multi-fold integral.

Lemma 5.25
.

Suppose
k
is a positive integer and
t>-1
. Then

\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{k}}\bigg|K^{(t)}_{z_{1}}(z_{2})\ldots K^{(t)}_{z_{k-1}}(z_{k})\bigg|\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{1})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{k})<\infty;

(5.13)

and for any
\epsilon>0
,

\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{k+1}}\bigg(\sum_{i,j}|1-\langle z_{i},z_{j}\rangle|\bigg)^{n+\epsilon}\bigg|K^{(t)}_{z_{0}}(z_{1})K^{(t)}_{z_{1}}(z_{2})\ldots K^{(t)}_{z_{k-1}}(z_{k})K^{(t)}_{z_{k}}(z_{0})\bigg|\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{0})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{k})<\infty.

(5.14)

Proof.

First, we notice that (
5.13
) is a special case of (
5.14
): take
\epsilon=t+1
, then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{k}}\bigg|K^{(t)}_{z_{1}}(z_{2})\ldots K^{(t)}_{z_{k-1}}(z_{k})\bigg|\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{1})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{k}}|1-\langle z_{1},z_{k}\rangle|^{n+t+1}\bigg|K^{(t)}_{z_{1}}(z_{2})\ldots K^{(t)}_{z_{k-1}}(z_{k})K^{(t)}_{z_{k}}(z_{1})\bigg|\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{1})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{k})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{k}}\bigg(\sum_{i,j}|1-\langle z_{i},z_{j}\rangle|\bigg)^{n+t+1}\bigg|K^{(t)}_{z_{1}}(z_{2})\ldots K^{(t)}_{z_{k-1}}(z_{k})K^{(t)}_{z_{k}}(z_{1})\bigg|\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{1})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{k}).

Thus it suffices to prove (
5.14
). It is well-known that all
l^{p}
norms on a finite set are equivalent. So by
[
44
, Proposition 5.1.2]
and Lemma
2.2
(7), we obtain the following estimates,

|1-\langle z_{i},z_{j}\rangle|=\bigg(|1-\langle z_{i},z_{j}\rangle|^{1/2}\bigg)^{2}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{s=0}^{k-1}|1-\langle z_{s},z_{s+1}\rangle|^{1/2}\bigg)^{2}\lesssim\sum_{s=0}^{k-1}|1-\langle z_{s},z_{s+1}\rangle|,\forall i,j,

and

\bigg(\sum_{i,j}|1-\langle z_{i},z_{j}\rangle|\bigg)^{n+\epsilon}\lesssim\bigg(\sum_{s=0}^{k-1}|1-\langle z_{s},z_{s+1}\rangle|\bigg)^{n+\epsilon}\lesssim\sum_{s=0}^{k-1}|1-\langle z_{s},z_{s+1}\rangle|^{n+\epsilon}.

Also, for any
s\geq 1
, take the rotation in variables
z_{s}\mapsto z_{0},z_{s+1}\mapsto z_{1},\ldots,z_{k}\mapsto z_{k-s},z_{0}\mapsto z_{k-s+1},z_{1}\mapsto z_{k-s+2},\ldots,z_{s-1}\mapsto z_{k}
, then we have the following equation,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{k+1}}|1-\langle z_{s},z_{s+1}\rangle|^{n+\epsilon}\bigg|K^{(t)}_{z_{0}}(z_{1})K^{(t)}_{z_{1}}(z_{2})\ldots K^{(t)}_{z_{k-1}}(z_{k})K^{(t)}_{z_{k}}(z_{0})\bigg|\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{0})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{k+1}}|1-\langle z_{0},z_{1}\rangle|^{n+\epsilon}\bigg|K^{(t)}_{z_{0}}(z_{1})K^{(t)}_{z_{1}}(z_{2})\ldots K^{(t)}_{z_{k-1}}(z_{k})K^{(t)}_{z_{k}}(z_{0})\bigg|\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{0})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{k}).

Notice that the part of the reproducing kernels is invariant under such a change of variable.
Thus it suffices to prove the integral above is finite. Without loss of generality, assume
0<\epsilon<1+t
. We prove it by induction. For
k=1
, by Lemma
2.3
(1), we compute the integral as follows.

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}|1-\langle z_{0},z_{1}\rangle|^{n+\epsilon}\bigg|K^{(t)}_{z_{0}}(z_{1})K^{(t)}_{z_{1}}(z_{0})\bigg|\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{0})\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}\frac{1}{|1-\langle z_{0},z_{1}\rangle|^{2(n+1+t)-n-\epsilon}}\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{0})\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}(1-|z_{1}|^{2})^{\epsilon-(1+t)}\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{1})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}(1-|z_{1}|^{2})^{\epsilon-1}\mathrm{d}m(z_{1})

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty.

Thus (
5.14
) holds for
k=1
. Suppose (
5.14
) holds for
k-1
. Then by Lemma
2.3
(2), we have the following estimates by the induction step.

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{k+1}}|1-\langle z_{0},z_{1}\rangle|^{n+\epsilon}\bigg|K^{(t)}_{z_{0}}(z_{1})K^{(t)}_{z_{1}}(z_{2})\ldots K^{(t)}_{z_{k-1}}(z_{k})K^{(t)}_{z_{k}}(z_{0})\bigg|\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{0})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{k}}\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\frac{1}{|1-\langle z_{0},z_{1}\rangle|^{1+t-\epsilon}|1-\langle z_{0},z_{k}\rangle|^{n+1+t}}\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{0})\bigg)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~\cdot\bigg|K^{(t)}_{z_{1}}(z_{2})\ldots K^{(t)}_{z_{k-1}}(z_{k})\bigg|\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{1})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{k})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{k}}\frac{1}{|1-\langle z_{1},z_{k}\rangle|^{1+t-\epsilon/2}}\bigg|K^{(t)}_{z_{1}}(z_{2})\ldots K^{(t)}_{z_{k-1}}(z_{k})\bigg|\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{1})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{k}}|1-\langle z_{1},z_{k}\rangle|^{n+\epsilon/2}\bigg|K^{(t)}_{z_{1}}(z_{2})\ldots K^{(t)}_{z_{k-1}}(z_{k})K^{(t)}_{z_{k}}(z_{1})\bigg|\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{1})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{k})

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty.

This completes the proof of Lemma
5.25
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.15
.

For simplicity of notation, in this proof let us write
A_{i}
for
\hat{A}_{i}
,
B_{i}
for
\hat{B}_{i}
, and
C_{i}
for
\hat{C}_{i}
.
The fact that
\{A_{i},B_{i}\}_{i=1}^{2n}
satisfy Hypotheses A follows from Lemma
5.14
and Lemma
5.24
. Thus by Proposition
5.9
, we have the following property, i.e.

[C_{1},C_{2},\ldots,C_{2n}]-[A_{1},A_{2},\ldots,A_{2n}]\in\mathcal{S}^{1},

and

[C_{1},C_{2},\ldots,C_{2n}]-[A_{1},A_{2},\ldots,A_{2n}]-\sum_{l=1}^{2n}[A_{1},\ldots,A_{l-1},B_{l},A_{l+1},\ldots,A_{2n}]

is a trace class operator with zero trace. Recall that

B_{i}=X_{f_{i}}+Y_{\bar{R}f_{i}}+K_{i},\quad i=1,2,\ldots,2n.

By Corollary
5.12
, each operator

[A_{1},\ldots,A_{l-1},K_{l},A_{l+1},\ldots,A_{2n}]

is in the trace class of zero trace. Denote

X=\sum_{l=1}^{2n}[A_{1},\ldots,A_{l-1},X_{f_{l}},A_{l+1},\ldots,A_{2n}],

and

Y=\sum_{l=1}^{2n}[A_{1},\ldots,A_{l-1},Y_{\bar{R}f_{l}},A_{l+1},\ldots,A_{2n}].

Then it follows from Proposition
5.9
that
X
and
Y
are trace class operators on
L_{a,t}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})
. It remains to show that they have zero trace.

Notation 5.26
.

For two functions
H(\xi),G(\xi)
on
\mathbb{B}_{n}
, write
H\simeq G
if
\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}(H(\xi)-G(\xi))\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(\xi)=0
.

By
[
47
, Lemma 2.5]
, it suffices to show

\langle XK^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle\simeq 0,\quad\text{and}\quad\langle YK^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle\simeq 0.

(5.15)

A moment of reflection shows that

X=\sum_{k=1}^{2n}\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)A_{\tau_{1}}\ldots A_{\tau_{k-1}}X_{f_{\tau_{k}}}A_{\tau_{k+1}}\ldots A_{\tau_{2n}},

and

Y=\sum_{k=1}^{2n}\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)A_{\tau_{1}}\ldots A_{\tau_{k-1}}Y_{\bar{R}f_{\tau_{k}}}A_{\tau_{k+1}}\ldots A_{\tau_{2n}}.

For
k=1,\ldots,2n
, denote

X_{k}=\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)A_{\tau_{1}}\ldots A_{\tau_{k-1}}X_{f_{\tau_{k}}}A_{\tau_{k+1}}\ldots A_{\tau_{2n}},

and

Y_{k}=\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)A_{\tau_{1}}\ldots A_{\tau_{k-1}}Y_{\bar{R}f_{\tau_{k}}}A_{\tau_{k+1}}\ldots A_{\tau_{2n}}.

Then we have the following expressions for
X
and
Y
,

X=\sum_{k=1}^{2n}X_{k},\quad Y=\sum_{k=1}^{2n}Y_{k}.

Define

X_{k}^{\prime}=\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)A_{\tau_{2n}}A_{\tau_{1}}\ldots A_{\tau_{k-1}}X_{f_{\tau_{k}}}A_{\tau_{k+1}}\ldots A_{\tau_{2n-1}},\quad k=1,\ldots,2n-1,

X_{2n}^{\prime}=\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)X_{f_{\tau_{2n}}}A_{\tau_{1}}\ldots A_{\tau_{2n-1}},

and

Y_{k}^{\prime}=\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)A_{\tau_{2n}}A_{\tau_{1}}\ldots A_{\tau_{k-1}}Y_{\bar{R}f_{\tau_{k}}}A_{\tau_{k+1}}\ldots A_{\tau_{2n-1}},\quad k=1,\ldots,2n-1,

Y_{2n}^{\prime}=\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)Y_{\bar{R}f_{\tau_{2n}}}A_{\tau_{1}}\ldots A_{\tau_{2n-1}}.

In other words,
X_{k}^{\prime},Y_{k}^{\prime}
are obtained from
X_{k},Y_{k}
by moving each rightmost operator to the leftmost.
Define

X^{\prime}=\sum_{k=1}^{2n}X_{k}^{\prime},\quad Y^{\prime}=\sum_{k=1}^{2n}Y_{k}^{\prime}.

The anti-symmetrization leads to

X^{\prime}=-X,\quad Y^{\prime}=-Y.

Below we show that

\langle XK^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle\simeq\langle X^{\prime}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle,\quad\langle YK^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle\simeq\langle Y^{\prime}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle.

This leads to (
5.15
), completing the proof of Proposition
5.15
.

Each
f_{i}
has a decomposition
f_{i}=g_{i}+h_{i}
, where
g_{i}
has compact support, and
h_{i}
has support away from
0
. Since
X_{g_{i}},Y_{\bar{R}g_{i}}
are perturbations that belong to the trace class, we might as well assume that each
f_{i}
have support away from the origin.

For
k,i=1,\ldots,2n
, write

X_{k,i}(w,z)=\begin{cases}X_{w}(z),&\text{ if }i=k,\\
K^{(t+1)}_{w}(z),&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

By definition, for any
k=1,\ldots,2n
, we compute
\langle X_{k}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle
as follows.

\displaystyle\langle X_{k}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg[\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{1}}}\ldots T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{k-1}}}X_{f_{\tau_{k}}}T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{k+1}}}\ldots T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{2n}}}K^{(t)}_{\xi}\bigg](\xi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2n}}f_{\tau_{1}}(z_{1})\ldots f_{\tau_{2n}}(z_{2n})K^{(t)}_{\xi}(z_{2n})

(5.16)

\displaystyle~~~~~\cdot X_{k,2n}(z_{2n},z_{2n-1})X_{k,2n-1}(z_{2n-1},z_{2n-2})\ldots X_{k,2}(z_{2},z_{1})X_{k,1}(z_{1},\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{2n})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2n}}\det\big[f_{i}(z_{j})\big]K^{(t)}_{\xi}(z_{2n})\bigg(\prod_{i=2}^{2n}X_{k,i}(z_{i},z_{i-1})\bigg)X_{k,1}(z_{1},\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{2n})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{1}).

Denote
F
the column vector of
2n
functions,

F(z)=[f_{1}(z)~f_{2}(z)~\ldots~f_{2n}(z)]^{T}.

Then by Lemma
2.2
, we estimate the determinant function,

\displaystyle\bigg|\det\big[f_{i}(z_{j})\big]\bigg|=

\displaystyle\bigg|\det\big[F(z_{1})~\ldots~F(z_{2n})\big]\bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg|\det\big[F(z_{1})~F(z_{2})-F(z_{1})~\ldots~F(z_{2n})-F(z_{1})\big]\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{i,j}|z_{i}-z_{j}|\bigg)^{2n-1}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{i,j}|1-\langle z_{i},z_{j}\rangle|\bigg)^{n-1/2}.

Also, we obtain the following estimate

|X_{k,i}(w,z)|\lesssim\begin{cases}|w|^{-2n+\epsilon}|K^{(t)}_{w}(z)|,&i=k,\\
|K^{(t+1)}_{w}(z)|=\frac{1}{|1-\langle z,w\rangle|}\cdot|K^{(t)}_{w}(z)|,&i\neq k.\end{cases}

Since we assume that each
f_{i}
is supported away from the origin, we continue computing the inner product
\langle X_{k}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle
using Lemma
2.2
and the above estimate.

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2n+1}}\bigg|\det[f_{i}(z_{j})]K^{(t)}_{\xi}(z_{2n})\bigg(\prod_{i=2}^{2n}X_{k,i}(z_{i},z_{i-1})\bigg)X_{k,1}(z_{1},\xi)\bigg|\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{2n})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{1})\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(\xi)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2n+1}}\bigg|\det[f_{i}(z_{j})]K^{(t)}_{\xi}(z_{2n})\bigg(\prod_{i\neq k,1}\frac{1-|z_{i}|^{2}}{|1-\langle z_{i},z_{i-1}\rangle|}\bigg)(1-|z_{k}|^{2})\frac{1-|z_{1}|^{2}}{|1-\langle z_{1},\xi\rangle|}

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~\cdot\bigg(\prod_{i=2}^{2n}K^{(t)}_{z_{i}}(z_{i-1})\bigg)K^{(t)}_{z_{1}}(\xi)\bigg|\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{2n})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{1})\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(\xi)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2n+1}}\bigg(\sum_{i,j}|1-\langle z_{i},z_{j}\rangle|\bigg)^{n+\frac{1}{2}}\bigg|K^{(t)}_{\xi}(z_{2n})\bigg(\prod_{i=2}^{2n}K^{(t)}_{z_{i}}(z_{i-1})\bigg)K^{(t)}_{z_{1}}(\xi)\bigg|\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{2n})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{1})\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(\xi)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty.

Here the last inequality follows from Lemma
5.25
.
Thus the
(2n+1)
-fold integral obtained by plugging (
5.3
) into
\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\langle X_{k}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(\xi)
converges absolutely. By Fubini’s Theorem, the variable
\xi
can be integrated first, and, since each
X_{k,i}(w,z)
is holomorphic in
z
, we compute the following integral

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\langle X_{k}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(\xi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\bigg\{\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2n}}\det\big[f_{i}(z_{j})\big]K^{(t)}_{\xi}(z_{2n})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\bigg(\prod_{i=2}^{2n}X_{k,i}(z_{i},z_{i-1})\bigg)X_{k,1}(z_{1},\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{2n})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{1})\bigg\}\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(\xi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2n}}\det\big[f_{i}(z_{j})\big]\bigg(\prod_{i=2}^{2n}X_{k,i}(z_{i},z_{i-1})\bigg)X_{k,1}(z_{1},z_{2n})\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{2n})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{1}).

By a similar proof, we have a similar expression for the integral of
\langle X_{k}^{\prime}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle
.

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\langle X_{k}^{\prime}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(\xi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\bigg\{\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2n}}\det[f_{i}(z_{j})]K^{(t)}_{\xi}(z_{2n-1})

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~~~~~~\cdot\bigg(\prod_{i=2}^{2n-1}X_{k,i}(z_{i},z_{i-1})\bigg)X_{k,1}(z_{1},z_{2n})X_{k,2n}(z_{2n},\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{2n})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{1})\bigg\}\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(\xi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2n}}\det[f_{i}(z_{j})]\bigg(\prod_{i=2}^{2n}X_{k,i}(z_{i},z_{i-1})\bigg)X_{k,1}(z_{1},z_{2n})\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{2n})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\langle X_{k}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(\xi).

In other words, we have shown the following equation

\langle X_{k}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle\simeq\langle X_{k}^{\prime}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle.

(5.17)

The situation for
Y
is more complicated. As in the case of
X
, for
k,i=1,\ldots,2n
, define

Y_{k,i}(w,z)=\begin{cases}Y_{w}(z),&i=k,\\
K^{(t+1)}_{w}(z),&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Then each
Y_{k,i}(w,z)
is holomorphic in
z
, and

|Y_{k,i}(w,z)|\lesssim\begin{cases}|w|^{-2n-1+\epsilon}|K^{(t)}_{w}(z)|,&i=k,\\
\frac{1}{|1-\langle w,z\rangle|}\cdot|K^{(t)}_{w}(z)|,&\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

By definition, we compute

\displaystyle\langle Y_{k}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2n}}\det\big[F(z_{1})~\ldots~F(z_{k-1})~\bar{R}F(z_{k})~F(z_{k+1})~\ldots~F(z_{2n})\big]

(5.18)

\displaystyle K^{(t)}_{\xi}(z_{2n})\bigg(\prod_{i=2}^{2n}Y_{k,i}(z_{i},z_{i-1})\bigg)Y_{k,1}(z_{1},\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{2n})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{1}).

Here
\bar{R}F(z)
is the column vector

\bar{R}F(z)=[\bar{R}f_{1}(z)~\ldots~\bar{R}f_{2n}(z)]^{T}.

Direct computation shows that

\displaystyle\det\big[F(z_{1})~\ldots~F(z_{k-1})~\bar{R}F(z_{k})~F(z_{k+1})~\ldots~F(z_{2n})\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\det\big[F(z_{1})-F(z_{k})~\ldots~F(z_{k-1})-F(z_{k})~\bar{R}F(z_{k})~F(z_{k+1})-F(z_{k})~\ldots~F(z_{2n})-F(z_{k})\big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{j\neq k}\det F_{j,k},

where
F_{j,k}
is the matrix function obtained by replacing the
j
-th column of

\big[F(z_{1})~\ldots~F(z_{k-1})~\bar{R}F(z_{k})~F(z_{k+1})~\ldots~F(z_{2n})\big]

into
F(z_{k})
.
Therefore we can compute its determinant as follows.

\displaystyle\det\big[F(z_{1})~\ldots~F(z_{k-1})~\bar{R}F(z_{k})~F(z_{k+1})~\ldots~F(z_{2n})\big]

(5.19)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\det\big[F(z_{1})-F(z_{k})~\ldots~F(z_{k-1})-F(z_{k})~\bar{R}F(z_{k})~F(z_{k+1})-F(z_{k})~\ldots~F(z_{2n})-F(z_{k})\big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{j\neq k}\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)f_{\tau_{j}}(z_{k})\bar{R}f_{\tau_{k}}(z_{k})\prod_{i\neq j,k}f_{\tau_{i}}(z_{i})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle D_{k}(z_{1},\ldots,z_{2n})+\sum_{j\neq k}E_{k,j}(z_{1},\ldots,z_{2n}),

where

\displaystyle D_{k}(z_{1},\ldots,z_{2n})=

\displaystyle\qquad\det\big[F(z_{1})-F(z_{k})~\ldots~F(z_{k-1})-F(z_{k})~\bar{R}F(z_{k})~F(z_{k+1})-F(z_{k})~\ldots~F(z_{2n})-F(z_{k})\big],

\displaystyle E_{k,j}(z_{1},\ldots,z_{2n})=\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)f_{\tau_{j}}(z_{k})\bar{R}f_{\tau_{k}}(z_{k})\prod_{i\neq j,k}f_{\tau_{i}}(z_{i}).

Correspondingly, we write

\langle Y_{k}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle=I_{k}(\xi)+\sum_{j\neq k}II_{k,j}(\xi),

where

I_{k}(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2n}}D_{k}(z_{1},\ldots,z_{2n})K^{(t)}_{\xi}(z_{2n})\bigg(\prod_{i=2}^{2n}Y_{k,i}(z_{i},z_{i-1})\bigg)Y_{k,1}(z_{1},\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{2n})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{1}),

and

II_{k,j}(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2n}}E_{k,j}(z_{1},\ldots,z_{2n})K^{(t)}_{\xi}(z_{2n})\bigg(\prod_{i=2}^{2n}Y_{k,i}(z_{i},z_{i-1})\bigg)Y_{k,1}(z_{1},\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{2n})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{1}).

Similarly, we have the following expression for
\langle Y_{k}^{\prime}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle
,

\langle Y_{k}^{\prime}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle=I_{k}^{\prime}(\xi)+\sum_{j\neq k}II_{k,j}^{\prime}(\xi),

where

\displaystyle I_{k}^{\prime}(\xi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2n}}D_{k}(z_{1},\ldots,z_{2n})K^{(t)}_{\xi}(z_{2n-1})\bigg(\prod_{i=2}^{2n-1}Y_{k,i}(z_{i},z_{i-1})\bigg)Y_{k,1}(z_{1},z_{2n})Y_{k,2n}(z_{2n},\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{2n})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{1}),

and

\displaystyle II_{k,j}^{\prime}(\xi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2n}}E_{k,j}(z_{1},\ldots,z_{2n})K^{(t)}_{\xi}(z_{2n-1})\bigg(\prod_{i=2}^{2n-1}Y_{k,i}(z_{i},z_{i-1})\bigg)Y_{k,1}(z_{1},z_{2n})Y_{k,2n}(z_{2n},\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{2n})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t+1}(z_{1}).

Since

\displaystyle|D_{k}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg|\det\big[F(z_{1})-F(z_{k})~\ldots~F(z_{k-1})-F(z_{k})~\bar{R}F(z_{k})~F(z_{k+1})-F(z_{k})~\ldots~F(z_{2n})-F(z_{k})\big]\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|z_{k}|\cdot\bigg(\sum_{i,j}|1-\langle z_{i},z_{j}\rangle|\bigg)^{n+1/2},

we can repeat the proof for (
5.17
) and get the identity

I_{k}(\xi)\simeq I_{k}^{\prime}(\xi).

(5.20)

Define

Z_{k,j}=\begin{cases}\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{1}}}\ldots\widehat{T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{j}}}}\ldots T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{k-1}}}Y_{f_{\tau_{j}}\bar{R}f_{\tau_{k}}}T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{k+1}}}\ldots T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{2n}}},&j<k\\
\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{1}}}\ldots T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{k-1}}}Y_{f_{\tau_{j}}\bar{R}f_{\tau_{k}}}T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{k+1}}}\ldots\widehat{T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{j}}}}\ldots T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{2n}}},&j>k,\end{cases}

and
Z_{k,j}^{\prime}
the operator obtained from
Z_{k,j}
by moving each rightmost operator to the leftmost. Here
\widehat{A}
means that
A
is removed. Checking by definition we get

II_{k,j}(\xi)=\langle Z_{k,j}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle,\quad II_{k,j}^{\prime}(\xi)=\langle Z_{k,j}^{\prime}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle.

Using antisymmetrization we see that

Z_{k,j}=\begin{cases}(-1)^{k-j-1}Z_{k,k-1},&j<k,\\
(-1)^{k-j-1}Z_{k,k+1},&j>k.\end{cases}

Therefore we have the following calculation.

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{2n}\sum_{j\neq k}Z_{k,j}=\sum_{k\text{ odd}}Z_{k,k+1}+\sum_{k\text{ even}}Z_{k,k-1}=\sum_{m=1}^{n}\bigg(Z_{2m-1,2m}+Z_{2m,2m-1}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{n}\bigg\{\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{1}}}\ldots T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{2m-2}}}Y_{f_{\tau_{2m}}\bar{R}f_{\tau_{2m-1}}}T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{2m+1}}}\ldots T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{2n}}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{1}}}\ldots T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{2m-2}}}Y_{f_{\tau_{2m-1}}\bar{R}f_{\tau_{2m}}}T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{2m+1}}}\ldots T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{2n}}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{n}\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{1}}}\ldots T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{2m-2}}}Y_{f_{\tau_{2m}\bar{R}f_{\tau_{2m-1}}}+f_{\tau_{2m-1}}\bar{R}f_{\tau_{2m}}}T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{2m+1}}}\ldots T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{2n}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{n}\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{1}}}\ldots T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{2m-2}}}Y_{\bar{R}[f_{\tau_{2m}}f_{\tau_{2m-1}}]}T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{2m+1}}}\ldots T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{\tau_{2n}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

Similarly,
\sum_{k=1}^{2n}\sum_{j\neq k}Z_{k,j}^{\prime}=0
.
Thus we can verify the following equations.

\sum_{k=1}^{2n}\sum_{j\neq k}II_{k,j}(\xi)=\sum_{k=1}^{2n}\sum_{j\neq k}\langle Z_{k,j}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle=\langle\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{2n}\sum_{j\neq k}Z_{k,j}\bigg)K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle=0,

\sum_{k=1}^{2n}\sum_{j\neq k}II_{k,j}^{\prime}(\xi)=\sum_{k=1}^{2n}\sum_{j\neq k}\langle Z_{k,j}^{\prime}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle=\langle\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{2n}\sum_{j\neq k}Z_{k,j}^{\prime}\bigg)K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle=0.

This leads to the following identities

\langle YK^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle=\sum_{k=1}^{2n}\langle Y_{k}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle=\sum_{k=1}^{2n}I_{k}(\xi)+\sum_{k=1}^{2n}\sum_{j\neq k}II_{k,j}(\xi)=\sum_{k=1}^{2n}I_{k}(\xi),

and

\langle Y^{\prime}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle=\sum_{k=1}^{2n}\langle Y_{k}^{\prime}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle=\sum_{k=1}^{2n}I_{k}^{\prime}(\xi)+\sum_{k=1}^{2n}\sum_{j\neq k}II_{k,j}^{\prime}(\xi)=\sum_{k=1}^{2n}I_{k}^{\prime}(\xi).

Combining with (
5.20
), we arrive at the following equation

\langle YK^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle\simeq\langle Y^{\prime}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle.

(5.21)

As explained before, (
5.17
) and (
5.21
) implies (
5.15
), which completes the proof of Proposition
5.15
.
∎

6
A Quantization Formula

Toeplitz quantization, or Berezin-Toeplitz quantization
[
4
]
, has been studied by many researchers on various types of domains. As an incomplete list, it was studied in
[
4
,
12
,
13
]
on the Fock space on
\mathbb{C}^{n}
; in
[
17
,
36
]
for planar domains; in
[
20
]
for pseudoconvex domains; in
[
24
,
49
]
on symmetric domains; in
[
18
,
39
,
40
,
45
]
for Kähler manifolds. Also see
[
1
,
21
,
45
]
for some very well-written surveys on this topic. In this section we give an explicit formula for the quantization of Toeplitz operators on the unit ball
\mathbb{B}_{n}
. In addition to the standard norm estimates, we also obtain Schatten class membership and Schatten norm estimates. The formulas and its corollaries will be important tools in the proof of Theorem
7.3
.

Recall that we defined the functions
d_{\alpha,\beta}
and
I^{\alpha,\beta}
in Definition
2.8
.

For
i=1,\ldots,n
, denote
e_{i}
the multi-index that equals
1
at the
i
-th entry and
0
elsewhere. For
i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k}
, denote

e_{i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{k}}=e_{i_{1}}+e_{i_{2}}+\ldots+e_{i_{k}}.

Theorem 6.1
.

Suppose
t>-1
,
k
is a non-negative integer and
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{k+1}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. Then we have the decomposition

T^{(t)}_{f}T^{(t)}_{g}=\sum_{l=0}^{k}c_{l,t}T^{(t)}_{C_{l}(f,g)}+R^{(t)}_{f,g,k+1},

(6.1)

where

c_{0,t}=1,~~c_{1,t}=nt^{-1}+O(t^{-2});\quad C_{0}(f,g)=fg,~~C_{1}(f,g)-C_{1}(g,f)=\frac{-i}{n}\{f,g\},

(6.2)

and

\|R_{f,g,k+1}^{(t)}\|\lesssim_{k}t^{-k-1}.

(6.3)

Here
\{f,g\}
is the Poisson bracket of
f
and
g
.

Moreover, the explicit formulas for
c_{l,t}
,
C_{l}(f,g)
and
R_{f,g,k+1}^{(t)}
are given as follows. For any
l\geq 0
,

c_{l,t}=\frac{\mathcal{F}^{(t)}_{n+l}\Phi_{n,l}^{(t)}(0)}{B(n,t+1)}\approx t^{-l},

(6.4)

C_{l}(f,g)(z)=(-1)^{l}(1-|z|^{2})^{-2l}\sum_{i_{1},j_{1},\ldots,i_{l},j_{l}=1}^{n}d_{e_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l}},e_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}}}(z)\bigg[D_{i_{l},j_{l}}\ldots D_{i_{1},j_{1}}(f(z)g(w))\bigg]\bigg|_{w=z},

(6.5)

where

D_{i,j}=(1-\langle z,w\rangle)^{2}\partial_{z_{i}}\bar{\partial}_{w_{j}}.

(6.6)

For any
h\in L_{a,t}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})
and
\xi\in\mathbb{B}_{n}
,

\displaystyle R_{f,g,k+1}^{(t)}h(\xi)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\Phi_{n,k+1}^{(t)}(|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2})S_{f,g,k+1}(z,w)h(w)K^{(t)}_{z}(\xi)K^{(t)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z),

(6.7)

where

\displaystyle S_{f,g,k+1}(z,w)=

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{k+1}}{|1-\langle w,z\rangle|^{2(k+1)}}

(6.8)

\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{i_{1},j_{1},\ldots,i_{k+1},j_{k+1}=1}^{n}I^{e_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k+1}},e_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k+1}}}(z-w)D_{i_{k+1},j_{k+1}}\ldots D_{i_{1},j_{1}}[f(z)g(w)].

The formulas above lead to Schatten-class membership and Schatten norm estimates of the remainder term.

Corollary 6.2
.

If
k\geq 0
and
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{k+1}(\overline{\mathbb{D}})
, then for any
p\geq 1
and
t>-1
,
R^{(t)}_{f,g,k+1}\in\mathcal{S}^{1}
. Moreover, for such
p
,

\|R^{(t)}_{f,g,k+1}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{k,p}t^{-(k+1)+\frac{n}{p}}.

Corollary 6.3
.

If
k\geq 0
and
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{k+1}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
, then for any
p>n
and
t>-1
,
R^{(t)}_{f,g,k+1}\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
. Moreover, for such
p
,

\|R^{(t)}_{f,g,k+1}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{k,p}t^{-(k+1)+\frac{n}{p}}.

Recall that

d_{e_{i},e_{j}}(z)=\int_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\big(A_{z}\zeta\big)_{i}\overline{\big(A_{z}\zeta\big)_{j}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma(\zeta)}{\sigma_{2n-1}}.

Therefore, we have the following formula for
C_{1}(f,g)
.

\begin{split}C_{1}(f,g)(z)=&-\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}d_{e_{i},e_{j}}(z)\partial_{i}f(z)\bar{\partial}_{j}g(z)\\
=&-\int_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(A_{z}\zeta\big)_{i}\partial_{i}f(z)\bigg)\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\overline{\big(A_{z}(\zeta)\big)_{j}}\bar{\partial}_{j}g(z)\bigg)\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma(\zeta)}{\sigma_{2n-1}}\end{split}

As in the proof of
[
47
, Equation (4.8)]
, the functions

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(A_{z}\zeta\big)_{i}\partial_{i}f(z)\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{j=1}^{n}\overline{\big(A_{z}(\zeta)\big)_{j}}\bar{\partial}_{j}g(z)=\overline{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\big(A_{z}(\zeta)\big)_{j}\partial_{j}\bar{g}(z)}

are independent of the choice of basis.
Thus
C_{1}(f,g)
is independent of the choice of basis. A similar computation shows that
C_{l}(f,g)
(
l=1,2,\cdots
) are all independent of the choice of basis for general
l
.

Remark 6.4
.

At
z\in\mathbb{B}_{n}
,
z\neq 0
, choose an orthonormal basis
\mathbf{e}_{z}=\{e_{z,1},e_{z,2},\ldots,e_{z,n}\}
of
\mathbb{C}^{n}
under which
z
has coordinates
(z_{1},0,\ldots,0)
. Then under
\mathbf{e}_{z}
, by (
2.7
),

C_{1}(f,g)(z)=C_{N}(f,g)(z)+C_{T}(f,g)(z),

where

C_{N}(f,g)(z)=-\frac{1}{n}(1-|z|^{2})^{2}\partial_{1}f(z)\bar{\partial}_{1}g(z),\quad C_{T}(f,g)(z)=-\frac{1}{n}(1-|z|^{2})\sum_{i=2}^{n}\partial_{i}f(z)\bar{\partial}_{i}g(z).

The functions
C_{N}(f,g)
and
C_{T}(f,g)
represent parts of
C_{1}(f,g)
involving derivatives of
f,g
in the complex normal and tangential directions, respectively. It is easy to see that the definitions of
C_{N}(f,g)
and
C_{T}(f,g)
do not depend on the choice of
\mathbf{e}_{z}
(as long as
e_{z,1}
is in
z
-direction). Locally, we can choose
\mathbf{e}_{z}
so that the vectors vary smoothly with respect to
z
. If
f,g
are
\mathscr{C}^{1}
except possibly at the origin. Also, it is easy to see from the definition that
C_{N}(f,g)
,
C_{T}(f,g)
are bounded. This implies

|C_{N}(f,g)(z)|\lesssim(1-|z|^{2})^{2},\quad|C_{T}(f,g)(z)|\lesssim 1-|z|^{2}.

By Lemma
4.8
, for
t
large enough, we obtain

T^{(t)}_{C_{N}(f,g)}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},\quad\|T^{(t)}_{C_{N}(f,g)}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{\frac{n}{p}},\quad\forall p>\frac{n}{2},p\geq 1,

and

T^{(t)}_{C_{T}(f,g)}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},\quad\|T^{(t)}_{C_{T}(f,g)}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{\frac{n}{p}},\quad\forall p>n,p\geq 1.

Remark 6.5
.

Continuing with Remark
6.4
, we can take
e_{z,1}=\frac{z}{|z|}
. Denote
\mathbf{e}=\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{n}\}
the canonical basis. Denote
\zeta_{i}=\zeta_{i}(\xi)

to be the
i
-th coordinate of
\xi
under the basis
\mathbf{e}_{z}
. Then compute

\frac{\partial\xi_{i}}{\partial\zeta_{1}}=\overline{\langle e_{i},e_{z,1}\rangle}=\big(e_{z,1}\big)_{i}=\frac{z_{i}}{|z|}.

Thus, we compute the following expressions,

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{\partial f(\xi)}{\partial\zeta_{1}}\frac{\partial g(\xi)}{\partial\bar{\zeta}_{1}}\bigg]\bigg|_{\xi=z}=

\displaystyle\bigg[\frac{\partial f(\xi)}{\partial\zeta_{1}}\frac{\partial g(\xi)}{\partial\bar{\zeta}_{1}}\bigg]\bigg|_{\xi=z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg[\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial f(\xi)}{\partial\xi_{i}}\frac{\partial\xi_{i}}{\partial\zeta_{1}}\frac{\partial g(\xi)}{\partial\bar{\xi}_{j}}\overline{\bigg(\frac{\partial\xi_{j}}{\partial\zeta_{1}}\bigg)}\bigg]\bigg|_{\xi=z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg[\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial f(\xi)}{\partial\xi_{i}}\frac{z_{i}}{|z|}\frac{\partial g(\xi)}{\partial\bar{\xi}_{j}}\overline{\bigg(\frac{z_{j}}{|z|}\bigg)}\bigg]\bigg|_{\xi=z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|z|^{-2}Rf(z)\bar{R}g(z),

and

\displaystyle\bigg[\sum_{i=2}^{n}\frac{\partial f(\xi)}{\partial\zeta_{i}}\frac{\partial g(\xi)}{\partial\bar{\zeta}_{j}}\bigg]\bigg|_{\xi=z}=

\displaystyle\langle\partial f(z),\bar{\partial}g(z)\rangle-|z|^{-2}Rf(z)\bar{R}g(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(\delta_{i,j}-\frac{z_{i}\bar{z}_{j}}{|z|^{2}}\big)\partial_{i}f(z)\bar{\partial}_{j}g(z).

In other words, we arrive at the following expression

C_{N}(f,g)(z)=-\frac{1}{n}(1-|z|^{2})^{2}|z|^{-2}Rf(z)\bar{R}g(z),

and

C_{T}(f,g)(z)=-\frac{1}{n}(1-|z|^{2})\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\big(\delta_{i,j}-\frac{z_{i}\bar{z}_{j}}{|z|^{2}}\big)\partial_{i}f(z)\bar{\partial}_{j}g(z).

Adding up the two equations gives the following formula,

C_{1}(f,g)(z)=-\frac{1}{n}(1-|z|^{2})\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\partial_{i}f(z)\bar{\partial}_{i}g(z)-Rf(z)\bar{R}g(z)\bigg].

Motivated by Remark
6.4
, we further decompose
R^{(t)}_{f,g,k+1}
according to the normal and tangential derivatives.

Definition 6.6
.

For
z\in\mathbb{B}_{n},z\neq 0
, let
\mathbf{e}_{z}=\{e_{z,1},e_{z,2},\ldots,e_{z,n}\}
be as in Remark
6.4
. Then
e_{z,1}
represents the complex normal direction at
z
, and
e_{z,2},\ldots,e_{z,n}
represents the complex tangential directions at
z
.

Under the basis
\mathbf{e}_{z}
, by (
6.8
),
S_{f,g,k+1}
decomposes into

S_{f,g,k+1}(z,w)=\sum_{1\leq|\alpha|,|\beta|\leq k+1}V_{k+1}^{\alpha,\beta}(z,w)\partial^{\alpha}f(z)\bar{\partial}^{\beta}g(w).

For integers
0\leq a,b\leq k+1
, define

S_{f,g,k+1}^{a,b}(z,w)=\sum_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle 1\leq|\alpha|,|\beta|\leq k+1}}{{\alpha_{1}=a,\beta_{1}=b}}}V_{k+1}^{\alpha,\beta}(z,w)\partial^{\alpha}f(z)\bar{\partial}^{\beta}g(w).

A moment of reflection shows that the function
S^{a,b}_{f,g,k+1}
does not depend on the choice of
\mathbf{e}_{z}
.
Define the corresponding operator on
L_{a,t}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})
,

\displaystyle R^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,k+1}h(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\Phi_{n,k+1}^{(t)}(|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2})S^{a,b}_{f,g,k+1}(z,w)h(w)K^{(t)}_{z}(\xi)K^{(t)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z).

Then we write

R^{(t)}_{f,g,k+1}=\sum_{a,b=0,\ldots,k+1}R^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,k+1}.

Corollary 6.7
.

Suppose
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{k+1}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. Then for any
0\leq a,b\leq k+1
any
p\geq 1
,
p>\max\{\frac{n}{1+\frac{a+b}{2}},\frac{n}{k+1+\frac{t+1}{2}}\}
,
R^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,k+1}\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
. Moreover, for such
p
, and
t
large enough,

\|R^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,k+1}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{k,p}t^{-k-1+\frac{n}{p}}.

In particular,

(1)

if one of
f,g
has the form
\phi(|z|^{2})
, where
\phi\in\mathscr{C}^{k+1}([0,1])
, then

R^{(t)}_{f,g,k+1}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},\quad\forall p>\max\{\frac{2n}{3},\frac{n}{k+1+\frac{t+1}{2}}\};

(2)

if both
f,g
are of the form
\phi(|z|^{2})
,
\phi\in\mathscr{C}^{k+1}([0,1])
, then

R^{(t)}_{f,g,k+1}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},\quad\forall p>\max\{\frac{n}{2},\frac{n}{k+1+\frac{t+1}{2}}\}.

For
p>\frac{2n}{3}
in case (1) and
p>\frac{n}{2}
in case (2) and
t
large enough,

\|R^{(t)}_{f,g,k+1}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{k,p}t^{-k-1+\frac{n}{p}}.

Corollary 6.8
.

Suppose
x,y
are positive integers, and
f\in\mathscr{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. Then the following hold.

(1)

For
p>\max\{\frac{n}{x+\frac{1}{2}},\frac{n}{1+\frac{t+1}{2}}\}
,
T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{x}}T^{(t)}_{f}-T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{x}f}
and
T^{(t)}_{f}T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{x}}-T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{x}f}
are in
\mathcal{S}^{p}
. For
p>\frac{n}{x+\frac{1}{2}}
and
t
large enough,

\|T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{x}}T^{(t)}_{f}-T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{x}f}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{-1+\frac{n}{p}},\quad\|T^{(t)}_{f}T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{x}}-T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{x}f}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{-1+\frac{n}{p}}.

(2)

For
p>\max\{\frac{n}{x+y},\frac{n}{1+\frac{t+1}{2}}\}
,
T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{x}}T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{y}}-T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{x+y}}\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
. For
p>\frac{n}{x+y}
and
t
large enough,

\|T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{x}}T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{y}}-T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{x+y}}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{-1+\frac{n}{p}}.

In general, we would expect that
T^{(t)}_{f}T^{(t)}_{g}-T^{(t)}_{fg}
have better Schatten class membership compared to
T^{(t)}_{f}T^{(t)}_{g}
: for arbitrary
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
,
T^{(t)}_{f}T^{(t)}_{g}
is only bounded, whereas the semi-commutator
T^{(t)}_{f}T^{(t)}_{g}-T^{(t)}_{fg}\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>n
. Also in case (1) of the corollary above, for a general function
f\in\mathscr{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
,
T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{x}}T^{(t)}_{f}
is in
\mathcal{S}^{p},\forall p>\frac{n}{x}
, while the semi-commutator
T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{x}}T^{(t)}_{f}-T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{x}f}
is in
\mathcal{S}^{p},\forall p>\frac{n}{x+\frac{1}{2}}.
This is no longer true in case (2): both
T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{x}}T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{y}}
and
T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{x}}T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{y}}-T^{(t)}_{(1-|z|^{2})^{x+y}}
are in
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for
p>\frac{n}{x+y}
. Intuitively, this has to do with the fact that functions of the form
(1-|z|^{2})^{x}
already vanishes along the radial direction to some order.

In the rest of this section, we prove Theorem
6.1
and Corollary
6.7
.

Functions of the form

\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l},j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}=1}^{n}I^{e_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l}},e_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}}}(z-w)D_{i_{l},j_{l}}D_{i_{l-1},j_{l-1}}\ldots D_{i_{1},j_{1}}[f(z)g(w)]

appear in the formula of
S_{f,g,k+1}(z,w)
in Theorem
6.1
. We need to estimate its absolute value. To start with, we write the above sum in terms of the standard derivation. Recall that

D_{i,j}=(1-\langle z,w\rangle)^{2}\partial_{z_{i}}\bar{\partial}_{w_{j}}.

Definition 6.9
.

Denote

A_{x,y}(z,w)=\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{x}=1}^{n}\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{y}=1}^{n}I^{e_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{x}},e_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{y}}}(z-w)\partial_{i_{1}}\ldots\partial_{i_{x}}f(z)\bar{\partial}_{j_{1}}\ldots\bar{\partial}_{j_{y}}g(w);

B_{1}(z,w)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}-w_{i})\partial_{z_{i}}(1-\langle z,w\rangle)=-\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}-w_{i})\bar{w}_{i}=\langle w-z,w\rangle;

B_{2}(z,w)=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\overline{(z_{j}-w_{j})}\bar{\partial}_{w_{j}}(1-\langle z,w\rangle)=\langle z,w-z\rangle;

C(z,w)=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}I^{e_{i},e_{j}}(z-w)\partial_{z_{i}}\bar{\partial}_{w_{j}}(1-\langle z,w\rangle)=-|z-w|^{2}.

In
D_{i_{l},j_{l}}D_{i_{l-1},j_{l-1}}\ldots D_{i_{2},j_{2}}
, the partial derivations
\partial_{z_{i_{l}}}
,
\bar{\partial}_{w_{j_{l}}},\ldots,\partial_{z_{i_{2}}}
,
\bar{\partial}_{w_{j_{2}}}
fall either on
f(z)g(w)
or a copy of
(1-\langle z,w\rangle)
. Thus the summation

\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l},j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}=1}^{n}I^{e_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l}},e_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}}}(z-w)D_{i_{l},j_{l}}D_{i_{l-1},j_{l-1}}\ldots D_{i_{1},j_{1}}[f(z)g(w)]

can be reorganized into sums of functions of the form

(1-\langle z,w\rangle)^{s_{1}}\big[C(z,w)\big]^{s_{2}}\big[B_{1}(z,w)\big]^{s_{3}}\big[B_{2}(z,w)\big]^{s_{4}}A_{x,y}(z,w).

In total, there are
2l
steps of taking partial derivatives, and there are
2l
copies of
(1-\langle z,w\rangle)
in the above. So

2s_{2}+s_{3}+s_{4}+x+y=s_{1}+s_{2}+s_{3}+s_{4}=2l.

Also, the partial derivatives of the first operator,
D_{i_{1},j_{1}}
, always apply on
f(z)g(w)
. So

x,y\geq 1.

From definition and Lemma
2.2
, the following estimates are obvious.

Lemma 6.10
.

Suppose
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{l}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. Then

(1)

|A_{x,y}(z,w)|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{x+y}|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{\frac{x+y}{2}}
;

(2)

|B_{1}(z,w)|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)||1-\langle z,w\rangle|
,
B_{2}(z,w)|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)||1-\langle z,w\rangle|;

(3)

|C(z,w)|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2}|1-\langle z,w\rangle|
.

Lemma 6.11
.

Suppose
k
is a non-negative integer and
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{k+1}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. For any
l=1,\ldots,k+1
, set

G_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l};j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}}(z,w)=D_{i_{l},j_{l}}D_{i_{l-1},j_{l-1}}\ldots D_{i_{1},j_{1}}[f(z)g(w)].

Then the following estimates hold.

\bigg|\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l},j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}=1}^{n}I^{e_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l}},e_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}}}(z-w)G_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l};j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}}(z,w)\bigg|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2l}|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{2l+1},

(6.9)

\bigg|\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l},j_{1},\ldots,j_{l},j_{l+1}=1}^{n}I^{e_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l}},e_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{l},j_{l+1}}}(z-w)\bar{\partial}_{w_{j_{l+1}}}G_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l};j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}}(z,w)\bigg|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2l+1}|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{2l+1}.

(6.10)

Proof.

As explained in Definition
6.9
, the following sum

\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l},j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}=1}^{n}I^{e_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l}},e_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}}}(z-w)G_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l};j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}}(z,w)

splits into sums of functions of the form

(1-\langle z,w\rangle)^{s_{1}}\big[C(z,w)\big]^{s_{2}}\big[B_{1}(z,w)\big]^{s_{3}}\big[B_{2}(z,w)\big]^{s_{4}}A_{x,y}(z,w),

with

x,y\geq 1,\quad 2s_{2}+s_{3}+s_{4}+x+y=s_{1}+s_{2}+s_{3}+s_{4}=2l.

Similarly, the sum

\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l},j_{1},\ldots,j_{l},j_{l+1}=1}^{n}I^{e_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l}},e_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{l},j_{l+1}}}(z-w)\bar{\partial}_{w_{j_{l+1}}}G_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l};j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}}(z,w)

splits into sums of functions of above form, with

x,y\geq 1,\quad 2s_{2}+s_{3}+s_{4}+x+y=2l+1,\quad s_{1}+s_{2}+s_{3}+s_{4}=2l.

By Lemma
6.10
, we have the following estimate

\displaystyle\bigg|(1-\langle z,w\rangle)^{s_{1}}\big[C(z,w)\big]^{s_{2}}\big[B_{1}(z,w)\big]^{s_{3}}\big[B_{2}(z,w)\big]^{s_{4}}A_{x,y}(z,w)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2s_{2}+s_{3}+s_{4}+x+y}|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{s_{1}+s_{2}+s_{3}+s_{4}+\frac{x+y}{2}}.

Plugging in the equations for
s_{i},x,y
gives the inequalities (
6.9
) and (
6.10
). This completes the proof of Lemma
6.11
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

Let
G_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l};j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}}
be defined as in Lemma
6.11
. Suppose
h\in\mathrm{Hol}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
and
\xi\in\mathbb{B}_{n}
. Write
F=f(z)g(w)h(w)K^{(t)}_{z}(\xi)
and

F_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l};j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}}=D_{i_{l},j_{l}}\ldots D_{i_{1},j_{1}}[f(z)g(w)h(w)K^{(t)}_{z}(\xi)]=G_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l};j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}}h(w)K^{(t)}_{z}(\xi).

Then we compute
T^{(t)}_{f}T^{(t)}_{g}
as follows.

\displaystyle T^{(t)}_{f}T^{(t)}_{g}h(\xi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}f(z)g(w)h(w)K^{(t)}_{z}(\xi)K^{(t)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}\Phi^{(t)}_{n,0}F(z,w)K^{(t)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z)

\displaystyle{\mathrel{\to}^{\eqref{eqn: formula F induction}}}

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{F}^{(t)}_{n}\Phi^{(t)}_{n,0}(0)}{B(n,t+1)}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}d_{0,0}(z)F(z,z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z)

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}\Phi^{(t)}_{n,1}(|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2})\frac{\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}I^{e_{i},e_{j}}(z-w)D_{i,j}F(z,w)}{|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{2}}K^{(t)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T^{(t)}_{fg}h(\xi)-\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}\Phi^{(t)}_{n,1}(|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2})\frac{\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}I^{e_{i},e_{j}}(z-w)F_{i,j}(z,w)}{|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{2}}K^{(t)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T^{(t)}_{fg}h(\xi)+R^{(t)}_{f,g,1}.

The condition for applying Lemma
2.13
is verified by Lemma
6.11
. In general, we have the following computation for
R^{(t)}_{f,g,l}
.

\displaystyle R^{(t)}_{f,g,l}h(\xi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{l}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}\Phi^{(t)}_{n,l}(|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2})\frac{\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l},j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}=1}^{n}I^{e_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l}},e_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}}}(z-w)F_{i_{l},\ldots,i_{1};j_{l},\ldots,j_{1}}}{|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{2l}}K^{(t)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(-1)^{l}\frac{\mathcal{F}^{(t)}_{n+l}\Phi^{(t)}_{n,l}(0)}{B(n,t+1)}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}(1-|z|^{2})^{-2l}\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l},j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}=1}^{n}d_{e_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l}},e_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}}}(z)F_{i_{l},\ldots,i_{1};j_{l},\ldots,j_{1}}(z,z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z)

\displaystyle+(-1)^{l+1}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(w)\Phi^{(t)}_{n,l+1}(|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2})K^{(t)}_{w}(z)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\cdot\frac{\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l+1},j_{1},\ldots,j_{l+1}=1}^{n}I^{e_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l+1},e_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{l+1}}}}(z-w)D_{i_{l+1},j_{l+1}}F_{i_{l},\ldots,i_{1};j_{l},\ldots,j_{1}}(z,w)}{|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{2(l+1)}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{l,t}T^{(t)}_{C_{l}(f,g)}h(\xi)+R^{(t)}_{f,g,l+1}h(\xi).

This proves the formulas in Theorem
6.1
for
h\in\mathrm{Hol}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. By
[
47
, Lemma 8.4]
, we have the following identity,

c_{0,t}=\frac{\mathcal{F}^{(t)}_{n}1(0)}{B(n,t+1)}=1.

The estimate (a) for
c_{l,t}
follows from Lemma
2.14
. The formulas for
C_{0}(f,g)
follows from direct computation. We can also see from the formula that
C_{l}(f,g)
does not depend on the choice of an orthonormal basis. Using
2.7
we directly verify that
C_{1}(f,g)-C_{1}(g,f)=\frac{-i}{n}\{f,g\}
. By Lemma
6.11
, we have the following estimates,

\begin{split}&|S_{f,g,k+1}(z,w)|\\
=&|1-\langle w,z\rangle|^{-2(k+1)}\bigg|\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k+1},j_{k+1},\ldots,j_{l}=1}^{n}I^{e_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k+1}},e_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k+1}}}(z-w)G_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k+1};j_{1},\ldots,j_{k+1}}(z,w)\bigg|\\
\lesssim&|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2(k+1)}|1-\langle z,w\rangle|.\end{split}

(6.11)

Thus it follows from Theorem
4.6
that
\|R^{(t)}_{f,g,k+1}\|\lesssim_{k}t^{-k-1}
.

By
[
47
, Lemma 5.3]
,

\mathcal{F}^{(t)}_{n+1}\Phi^{(t)}_{n,1}(0)=n!t^{-n-1}+o(t^{-n-2})

as
t
tends to infinity.
Therefore, we arrive at the following estimate

c_{1,t}=\frac{\mathcal{F}^{(t)}_{n+1}\Phi_{n,1}^{(t)}(0)}{B(n,t+1)}=\frac{B(n+1,t+1)}{B(n,t+1)}+O(\frac{B(n+2,t+1)}{B(n,t+1)})=nt^{-1}+O(t^{-2}).

This completes the proof of Theorem
6.1
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
6.2
.

In the case when
n=1
, the estimates in the proof of Lemma
6.10
are improved into

(1)

|A_{x,y}(z,w)|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{x+y}|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{x+y}
;

(2)

|B_{1}(z,w)|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)||1-\langle z,w\rangle|
,
B_{2}(z,w)|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)||1-\langle z,w\rangle|
;

(3)

|C(z,w)|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2}|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{2}
.

This leads to

\bigg|\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l},j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}=1}^{n}I^{e_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l}},e_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}}}(z-w)G_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{l};j_{1},\ldots,j_{l}}(z,w)\bigg|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2l}|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{2l+2}

(6.12)

and then

|S_{f,g,k+1}(z,w)|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2(k+1)}|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{2}.

The corollary follows from Theorem
4.6
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
6.3
.

The corollary follows from (
6.11
) and Theorem
4.6
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
6.7
.

As in Definition
6.6
, for
z\in\mathbb{B}_{n},z\neq 0
, let
\mathbf{e}_{z}
be an orthonormal basis of
\mathbb{C}^{n}
so that
e_{z,1}=\frac{z}{|z|}
. Under the basis
\mathbf{e}_{z}
, for
0\leq a,b\leq k+1
,
S^{a,b}_{f,g,k+1}(z,w)
consists of the part of
S_{f,g,k+1}
that contains
\partial^{\alpha}f(z)\bar{\partial}^{\beta}g(w)
with
\alpha_{1}=a,\beta_{1}=b
.

Since
R^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,k+1}=P^{(t)}T^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,k+1}
, where

T^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,k+1}h(z)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\Phi^{(t)}_{n,k+1}(|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2})S^{a,b}_{f,g,k+1}(z,w)h(w)K^{(t)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(w).

To prove the Schatten class membership of the
R^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,k+1}
operators, it amounts to prove the corresponding estimates for the kernel
S^{a,b}_{f,g,k+1}(z,w)
, and apply Theorem
4.6
.

Locally choose the basis
\mathbf{e}_{z}
so that it varies smooth with respect to
z
.
Under the basis
\mathbf{e}_{z}
, define

A_{\alpha,\beta}(z,w)=I^{\alpha,\beta}(z-w)\partial^{\alpha}f(z)\bar{\partial}^{\beta}g(w).

Then by Lemma
2.2
, we have the following bound,

|A_{\alpha,\beta}(z,w)|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{|\alpha|+|\beta|}|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{\frac{|\alpha|+|\beta|+\alpha_{1}+\beta_{1}}{2}}.

Then
S^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,k+1}(z,w)
is a finite linear combination of terms like:

\frac{1}{|1-\langle w,z\rangle|^{2(k+1)}}\cdot(1-\langle z,w\rangle)^{s_{1}}\big[C(z,w)\big]^{s_{2}}\big[B_{1}(z,w)\big]^{s_{3}}\big[B_{2}(z,w)\big]^{s_{4}}A_{\alpha,\beta}(z,w),

where
|\alpha|,|\beta|\geq 1,2s_{2}+s_{3}+s_{4}+|\alpha|+|\beta|=s_{1}+s_{2}+s_{3}+s_{4}=2(k+1),
and
\alpha_{1}=a,\beta_{1}=b
. Therefore we obtain the following estimate

\displaystyle|S^{a,b}_{f,g,k+1}(z,w)|\lesssim

\displaystyle|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2s_{2}+s_{3}+s_{4}+|\alpha|+|\beta|}|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{-2(k+1)+s_{1}+s_{2}+s_{3}+s_{4}+\frac{|\alpha|+|\beta|+\alpha_{1}+\beta_{1}}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2(k+1)}|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{\frac{2+a+b}{2}}.

Thus by Theorem
4.6
,
R^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,k+1}=P^{(t)}T^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,k+1}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},\forall p>\max\{\frac{n}{1+\frac{a+b}{2}},\frac{n}{k+1+\frac{t+1}{2}}\}
, and for such
p
, we have

\|R^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,k+1}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{k,p}t^{-k-1+\frac{n}{p}}.

If
f=\phi(|z|^{2})
for some
\phi
, then
R^{(t)0,b}_{f,g,k+1}=0
for any
b
. If both
f,g
are of such form then
R^{(t)a,0}_{f,g,k+1},R^{(t)0,b}_{f,g,k+1}=0
for any
a,b
. This gives the improved Schatten-class membership in (1) and (2), and proves Corollary
6.7
.

∎

Proof of Corollary
6.8
.

By Theorem
6.1
, for
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
,
T^{(t)}_{f}T^{(t)}_{g}-T^{(t)}_{fg}=R^{(t)}_{f,g,1}
, where

R^{(t)}_{f,g,1}h(z)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}\Phi^{(t)}_{n,1}(|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2})S_{f,g,1}(z,w)h(w)K^{(t)}_{z}(\xi)K^{(t)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z),

with

S_{f,g,1}(z,w)=\frac{-(1-\langle z,w\rangle)^{2}}{|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{2}}\langle\partial f(z),\overline{z-w}\rangle\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle.

If
f(z)=(1-|z|^{2})^{x}
then we have the following estimate

\big|\langle\partial f(z),\overline{z-w}\rangle\big|=\big|(x-1)(1-|z|^{2})^{x-1}\langle\bar{z},\overline{z-w}\rangle\big|\lesssim(1-|z|^{2})^{x-1}|1-\langle z,w\rangle|\lesssim|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{x}.

Thus we arrive at the estimate,

\big|\langle\partial f(z),\overline{z-w}\rangle\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle\big|\lesssim\begin{cases}|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{x+1/2},&\text{ if one of $f$ or $g$ equals }(1-|z|^{2})^{x}\\
|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{x+y},&\text{ if }f=(1-|z|^{2})^{x},g(z)=(1-|z|^{2})^{y}.\end{cases}

Corollary
6.8
follows from the above inequality and Theorem
4.6
. This completes the proof of Corollary
6.8
.
∎

7
Even and Odd Antisymmetrizations

As explained in the introduction, the goal of this section is to prove the trace class membership of the antisymmetric sum
[T^{(t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t)}_{f_{2n}}]
as well as the asymptotic trace formula (
1.7
). To begin with, we define even and odd partial antisymmetrizations, which are generalizations of semi-commutators.

Definition 7.1
.

For
f,g\in C(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
and
t\geq-1
, denote the semi-commutator on
L_{a,t}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})
,

\sigma_{t}(f,g)=T^{(t)}_{f}T^{(t)}_{g}-T^{(t)}_{fg}.

(7.1)

For
f_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}\in C(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
and
t\geq-1
, define the following partial anti-symmetric sums.

[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]^{\mathrm{odd}}_{t}=\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)\sigma_{t}(f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1})\ldots\sigma_{t}(f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n}),

(7.2)

and

[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]^{\mathrm{even}}_{t}=\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)\sigma_{t}(f_{1},g_{\tau_{1}})\ldots\sigma_{t}(f_{n},g_{\tau_{n}}).

(7.3)

In the case when
n=1
, the operators above both agree with the semi-commutator
\sigma_{t}(f,g)
. In higher dimensions, the partial anti-symmetric sum (
7.2
) and (
7.3
) generalize the semi-commutator (
7.1
).

Remark 7.2
.

Take the case
t>-1
for example. Under the decomposition
L^{2}(\lambda_{t})=L_{a,t}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})\oplus L_{a,t}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})^{\perp}
, we can write a multiplication operator
M_{f}
as a block matrix

M_{f}=\begin{bmatrix}T^{(t)}_{f}&H^{(t)*}_{\bar{f}}\\
H^{(t)}_{f}&S^{(t)}_{f}\end{bmatrix},

where
T^{(t)}_{f},H^{(t)}_{f}
and
S^{(t)}_{f}
are Toeplitz operator, Hankel operator, and dual Toeplitz operator, respectively. From the equations

\sigma_{t}(f,g)=-H^{(t)*}_{\bar{f}}H^{(t)}_{g},

and

[T^{(t)}_{f},T^{(t)}_{g}]=\sigma_{t}(f,g)-\sigma_{t}(g,f)=H^{(t)*}_{\bar{g}}H^{(t)}_{f}-H^{(t)*}_{\bar{f}}H^{(t)}_{g},

we can see that both operators come from products of off-diagonal terms under the above block matrix representation. Under this point of view, the even and odd antisymmetrizations defined above are linear combinations of alternating products of off-diagonal terms, with
f_{i}
appearing in the top right, and
g_{j}
appearing in the bottom left.

Theorem 7.3
.

Suppose
t\geq-1
and
f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
.
Then the partial antisymmetrizations
[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]_{t}^{\mathrm{odd}}
and
[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]_{t}^{\mathrm{even}}
are in the trace class. Moreover,

\lim_{t\to\infty}\mathrm{Tr}[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]_{t}^{\mathrm{odd}}=\lim_{t\to\infty}\mathrm{Tr}[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]_{t}^{\mathrm{even}}=\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\partial f_{1}\wedge\bar{\partial}g_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\partial f_{n}\wedge\bar{\partial}g_{n}.

(7.4)

As an application of the above theorem, we obtain the following asymptotic estimate for the Schatten-
4
norm of Hankel operators at dimension
2
. We can view it as a higher uncertainty principle.

Corollary 7.4
.

Suppose
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{2}})
are supported inside
\mathbb{B}_{2}
. Then the infimum limit

\varliminf\limits_{t\to\infty}\|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{1}}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{4}}^{2}\|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{2}}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{4}}^{2}

satisfies

\varliminf_{t\to\infty}\|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{1}}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{4}}^{2}\|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{2}}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{4}}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{2}}\partial f_{1}\wedge\partial f_{2}\wedge\overline{\partial f_{1}\wedge\partial f_{2}}.

Proof.

Take
g_{1}=\bar{f}_{1}
,
g_{2}=\bar{f}_{2}
. By definition, we compute the expression of
[f_{1},\bar{f}_{1},f_{2},\bar{f}_{2}]^{\mathrm{even}}
,

\displaystyle[f_{1},\bar{f}_{1},f_{2},\bar{f}_{2}]^{\mathrm{even}}=

\displaystyle\sigma_{t}(f_{1},\bar{f}_{1})\sigma_{t}(f_{2},\bar{f}_{2})-\sigma_{t}(f_{1},\bar{f}_{2})\sigma_{t}(f_{2},\bar{f}_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle H^{(t)*}_{\bar{f}_{1}}H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{1}}H^{(t)*}_{\bar{f}_{2}}H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{2}}-H^{(t)*}_{\bar{f}_{1}}H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{2}}H^{(t)*}_{\bar{f}_{2}}H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{1}}|^{2}|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{2}}|^{2}-\bigg(H^{(t)*}_{\bar{f}_{2}}H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{1}}\bigg)^{*}H^{(t)*}_{\bar{f}_{2}}H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{1}}.

Then by Theorem
7.3
, we have the following limit,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}[f_{1},\bar{f}_{1},f_{2},\bar{f}_{2}]^{\mathrm{even}}=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{1}}|^{2}|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{2}}|^{2}-\mathrm{Tr}\bigg(H^{(t)*}_{\bar{f}_{2}}H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{1}}\bigg)^{*}H^{(t)*}_{\bar{f}_{2}}H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{1}}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{2}}\partial f_{1}\wedge\bar{\partial}\bar{f}_{1}\wedge\partial f_{2}\wedge\bar{\partial}\bar{f}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{2}}\partial f_{1}\wedge\partial f_{2}\wedge\overline{\partial f_{1}\wedge\partial f_{2}},\quad t\to\infty.

Notice that
\bigg(H^{(t)*}_{\bar{f}_{2}}H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{1}}\bigg)^{*}H^{(t)*}_{\bar{f}_{2}}H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{1}}\geq 0
. The Cauchy-Schwartz inequality gives the following bound,

\bigg|\mathrm{Tr}|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{1}}|^{2}|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{2}}|^{2}\bigg|=\bigg|\langle|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{2}}|^{2},|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{1}}|^{2}\rangle_{\mathcal{S}^{2}}\bigg|\leq\langle|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{2}}|^{2},|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{2}}|^{2}\rangle_{\mathcal{S}^{2}}^{1/2}\langle|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{1}}|^{2},|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{1}}|^{2}\rangle_{\mathcal{S}^{2}}^{1/2}=\|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{2}}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{4}}^{2}\|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{1}}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{4}}^{2}.

We have the following bound from the above computation

\varliminf_{t\to\infty}\|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{2}}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{4}}^{2}\|H^{(t)}_{\bar{f}_{1}}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{4}}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{4\pi^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{2}}\partial f_{1}\wedge\partial f_{2}\wedge\overline{\partial f_{1}\wedge\partial f_{2}}.

This completes the proof of Corollary
7.4
.
∎

Remark 7.5
.

Both
[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]^{\mathrm{even}}_{t}
and
[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]^{\mathrm{odd}}_{t}
are sums of compositions of
n
semi-commutators, each belonging to
\mathcal{S}^{p},\forall p>n
. This proves that they belong to
\mathcal{S}^{p}
,
\forall p>1
. However, the trace class membership of these operators relies on some higher order cancellation and is therefore nontrivial. As an example, we can show by direct computation that each
\sigma_{t}(z_{i},\bar{z}_{i})
is the diagonal operator under the basis
\{\frac{z^{\alpha}}{\|z^{\alpha}\|}\}_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n}}
, with entry
-\frac{|\alpha|-\alpha_{i}+n+t}{(n+|\alpha|+t)(n+|\alpha|+t+1)}
at
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n}
. Therefore
\sigma_{t}(z_{i},\bar{z}_{i})\notin\mathcal{S}^{n}
. (In fact, in
[
51
]
it was proved that for
f\in H^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}_{n})
,
\sigma_{0}(f,\bar{f})\in\mathcal{S}^{n}
if and only if
f
is constant.) However, the theorem states that

[z_{1},\bar{z}_{1},\ldots,z_{n},\bar{z}_{n}]^{\mathrm{odd}}_{t}=\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)\sigma_{t}(z_{\tau_{1}},\bar{z}_{1})\ldots\sigma_{t}(z_{\tau_{n}},\bar{z}_{n})\in\mathcal{S}^{1}.

The partial anti-symmetrization needs to be carefully chosen so that the higher cancellation works. For example,

\sigma_{t}(z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})\ldots\sigma_{t}(z_{n},\bar{z}_{n})-\sigma_{t}(\bar{z}_{1},z_{2})\sigma_{t}(\bar{z}_{2},z_{3})\ldots\sigma_{t}(\bar{z}_{n},z_{1})=\sigma_{t}(z_{1},\bar{z}_{1})\ldots\sigma_{t}(z_{n},\bar{z}_{n})\notin\mathcal{S}^{1}.

Therefore simply taking anti-symmetrization over a rotation generally does not give a trace class operator. The above example also shows that the Connes-Chern character (
1.2
) for the Toeplitz extension is in general not well-defined at
p=n
. At
p=n+1
, by Theorem
6.1
, for
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
,

\|\sigma_{t}(f,g)\|_{\mathcal{S}^{n+1}}=\|R^{(t)}_{f,g,1}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{n+1}}\lesssim t^{-1+\frac{n}{n+1}}=t^{-\frac{1}{n+1}}.

Therefore for
f_{0},\ldots,f_{2n+1}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
, the Connes-Chern character at
p=n+1
satisfies

|\tau_{t}(f_{0},\ldots,f_{2p-1})|\lesssim t^{-1}\to 0,\quad t\to\infty.

In particular, its value depends on
t
. In Subsection
8.2
, we consider the Connes-Chern character at
p>n
after multiplying a suitable power of
t
.

Theorem
7.3
immediately leads to the trace class membership and asymptotic trace formula for the full anti-symmetric sum, which consist an important part of the proof of our main result, Theorem
8.1
.

Corollary 7.6
.

Suppose
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{2n}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
and
t\geq-1
. Then
[T^{(t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t)}_{f_{2n}}]
is in the trace class
\mathcal{S}^{1}
. Moreover,

\lim_{t\to\infty}\mathrm{Tr}[T^{(t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t)}_{f_{2n}}]=\frac{n!}{(2\pi i)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\mathrm{d}f_{1}\wedge\mathrm{d}f_{2}\wedge\ldots\wedge\mathrm{d}f_{2n}.

(7.5)

Proof.

By Remark
7.2
, it is easy to see the following identity

[T^{(t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t)}_{f_{2n}}]=\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)[f_{\tau_{1}},f_{\tau_{2}},\ldots,f_{\tau_{2n}}]^{\mathrm{odd}}.

Therefore it follows from Theorem
7.3
that
[T^{(t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t)}_{f_{2n}}]\in\mathcal{S}^{1}
. This proves the trace class membership.

Let
H
be the collection of subsets of
\{1,\ldots,2n\}
consisting of
n
elements. For each
a\in H
, let
[a]
be the subset of
S_{2n}
that sends
\{1,3,\ldots,2n-1\}
to
a
. Then
\#[a]=(n!)^{2}
for any
a\in H
. In each
[a]
there is a unique permutation
\tau_{a}
that satisfies

\tau_{a}(1)<\tau_{a}(3)<\ldots<\tau_{a}(2n-1),\quad\tau_{a}(2)<\tau_{a}(4)<\ldots<\tau_{a}(2n).

Then we compute the limit of the trace of full antisymmetrization as follows,

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}\mathrm{Tr}[T^{(t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t)}_{f_{2n}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\partial f_{\tau_{1}}\wedge\bar{\partial}f_{\tau_{2}}\wedge\ldots\wedge\partial f_{\tau_{2n-1}}\wedge\bar{\partial}f_{\tau_{2n}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{n}n!}\sum_{a\in H}\sum_{\tau\in[a]}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\partial f_{\tau_{1}}\wedge\bar{\partial}f_{\tau_{2}}\wedge\ldots\wedge\partial f_{\tau_{2n-1}}\wedge\bar{\partial}f_{\tau_{2n}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{n}n!}\sum_{a\in H}(n!)^{2}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau_{a})\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\partial f_{\tau_{a}(1)}\wedge\bar{\partial}f_{\tau_{a}(2)}\wedge\ldots\wedge\partial f_{\tau_{a}(2n-1)}\wedge\bar{\partial}f_{\tau_{a}(2n)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n!}{(2\pi i)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\mathrm{d}f_{1}\wedge\mathrm{d}f_{2}\wedge\ldots\wedge\mathrm{d}f_{2n}.

This proves (
7.5
).
∎

7.1
Proof of Theorem
7.3
(
t>-1
)

Since
\sigma_{t}(f,g)=\bigg[\sigma_{t}(\bar{g},\bar{f})\bigg]^{*}
, we can verify that

[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]_{t}^{\mathrm{odd}}=\bigg([\bar{g}_{n},\bar{f_{n}},\ldots,\bar{g}_{1},\bar{f_{1}}]_{t}^{\mathrm{even}}\bigg)^{*}.

Thus it suffices to prove the results for the odd partial anti-symmetric sums.

Notation 7.7
.

For two operators
A
and
B
, temporarily denote
A\sim_{w}B
if
A-B
is a trace class operator. If
A_{t}
and
B_{t}
are parameterized families of operators on
L_{a,t}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})
, temporarily denote
A_{t}\sim_{a}B_{t}
if
A_{t}-B_{t}
are trace class operators on
L_{a,t}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})
with trace norm tending to zero as
t\to\infty
.

Part 1:
We prove

[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]_{t}^{\mathrm{odd}}\in\mathcal{S}^{1},\forall t>-1.

By Theorem
6.1
and Corollary
6.7
, for
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
, recall

\sigma_{t}(f,g)=R^{(t)}_{f,g,1},

and

R^{(t)}_{f,g,1}=\sum_{a,b=0,1}R^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,1},

where

R^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,1}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},\forall p>\max\bigg\{\frac{n}{1+\frac{a+b}{2}},\frac{n}{1+\frac{t+1}{2}}\bigg\}.

In particular, if one of
a,b
is non-zero, then
R^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,1}\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
for some
p<n
. It follows immediately that for
f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
,
\tau\in S_{n}
,

\sigma_{t}(f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1})\sigma_{t}(f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2})\ldots\sigma_{t}(f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n})\sim_{w}R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},1}R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2},1}\ldots R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n},1}.

Thus in order to prove
[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]^{\mathrm{odd}}_{t}\in\mathcal{S}^{1}
, it remains to prove

\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},1}R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2},1}\ldots R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n},1}\in\mathcal{S}^{1}.

(7.6)

Recall that by definition the integral kernel of
R^{(t)0,0}_{f,g,1}
consists of the part that involves only complex tangential derivatives of
f
and
g
. Tracing back in Theorem
6.1
we have the following formula

\displaystyle R^{(t)0,0}_{f,g,1}h(\xi)=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}\Phi^{(t)}_{n,1}(|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2})V_{1}(z,w)\langle Q_{\bar{z}}\partial f(z),\overline{z-w}\rangle\langle Q_{z}\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle

\displaystyle~~~~~~~\cdot h(w)K^{(t)}_{z}(\xi)K^{(t)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z),

where
V_{1}(z,w)=\frac{1-\langle z,w\rangle}{1-\langle w,z\rangle}
is bounded.
For any
j=1,\ldots,n
and
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
, define
