F_{\Lambda_{g,1,j}}(z,w)=-V_{1}(z,w)(z_{j}-w_{j})\langle Q_{z}\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle K^{(t)}_{w}(z),\quad z,w\in\mathbb{B}_{n}.

Define the operator

\displaystyle\Lambda_{g,1,j}h(z)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\Phi_{n,1}^{(t)}(|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2})F_{\Lambda_{g,1,j}}(z,w)h(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(w).

(7.7)

Let
[Q_{\bar{z}}\partial f(z)]_{j}
be the
j
-th entry of the vector
Q_{\bar{z}}\partial f(z)
. Then by the above expressions, we have the following expression

R^{(t)0,0}_{f,g,1}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}P^{(t)}M^{(t)}_{[Q_{\bar{z}}\partial f]_{j}}\Lambda_{g,1,j}.

By Lemma
2.2
we have the bound

\big|F_{\Lambda_{g,1,i}}(z,w)\big|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2}\frac{1}{|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{n+1+t-1}},\quad z,w\in\mathbb{B}_{n}.

Thus by Theorem
4.6
and Corollary
4.7
, for any Lipschitz function
u
on
\mathbb{B}_{n}
,
j=1,\ldots,n
, the following hold.

•

Both
P^{(t)}\Lambda_{g,1,j}
and
\Lambda_{g,1,j}P^{(t)}
are in
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>n
, with Schatten-
p
norm
\lesssim_{p}t^{-1+\frac{n}{p}}
.

•

For any Lipschitz function
u
, both
P^{(t)}[\Lambda_{g,1,j},M^{(t)}_{u}]
and
[\Lambda_{g,1,j},M^{(t)}_{u}]P^{(t)}
are in
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>\max\{\frac{2n}{3},\frac{n}{1+\frac{t+1}{2}}\}
, with Schatten-
p
norm
\lesssim_{p}t^{-1+\frac{n}{p}}
. In particular, these operators belong to
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for some
p<n
.

Also, by Corollary
4.3
, we have

•

For any Lipschitz function
u
,
[P^{(t)},M_{u}^{(t)}]\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>2n
.

Denote

u_{i,j}(z)=[Q_{\bar{z}}\partial f_{i}(z)]_{j},\quad i,j=1,\ldots,n.

Then by the above discussion, we compute the product of semi-commutators as follows.

\displaystyle\sigma_{t}(f_{1},g_{1})\sigma_{t}(f_{2},g_{2})\ldots\sigma_{t}(f_{n},g_{n})

\displaystyle\sim_{w}

\displaystyle R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{1},g_{1},1}R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{2},g_{2},1}\ldots R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{n},g_{n},1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}P^{(t)}M^{(t)}_{u_{1,j_{1}}}\Lambda_{g_{1},1,j_{1}}P^{(t)}M^{(t)}_{u_{2,j_{2}}}\Lambda_{g_{2},1,j_{2}}\ldots P^{(t)}M^{(t)}_{u_{n,j_{n}}}\Lambda_{g_{n},1,i_{n}}P^{(t)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}P^{(t)}\Lambda_{g_{1},1,j_{1}}P^{(t)}M^{(t)}_{u_{1,j_{1}}}M^{(t)}_{u_{2,j_{2}}}\Lambda_{g_{2},1,j_{2}}\ldots P^{(t)}M^{(t)}_{u_{n,j_{n}}}\Lambda_{g_{n},1,i_{n}}P^{(t)}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}P^{(t)}[M^{(t)}_{u_{1,j_{1}}},\Lambda_{g_{1},1,j_{1}}]P^{(t)}M^{(t)}_{u_{2,j_{2}}}\Lambda_{g_{2},1,j_{2}}\ldots P^{(t)}M^{(t)}_{u_{n,j_{n}}}\Lambda_{g_{n},1,i_{n}}P^{(t)}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}P^{(t)}\Lambda_{g_{1},1,j_{1}}[M^{(t)}_{u_{1,j_{1}}},P^{(t)}]M^{(t)}_{u_{2,j_{2}}}\Lambda_{g_{2},1,j_{2}}\ldots P^{(t)}M^{(t)}_{u_{n,j_{n}}}\Lambda_{g_{n},1,i_{n}}P^{(t)}

\displaystyle\sim_{w}

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}P^{(t)}\Lambda_{g_{1},1,j_{1}}P^{(t)}M^{(t)}_{u_{1,j_{1}}}M^{(t)}_{u_{2,j_{2}}}\Lambda_{g_{2},1,j_{2}}\ldots P^{(t)}M^{(t)}_{u_{n,j_{n}}}\Lambda_{g_{n},1,i_{n}}P^{(t)}.

Continuing like this, we obtain the following expression

\displaystyle\sigma_{t}(f_{1},g_{1})\sigma_{t}(f_{2},g_{2})\ldots\sigma_{t}(f_{n},g_{n})

\displaystyle\sim_{w}

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}P^{(t)}\Lambda_{g_{1},1,j_{1}}P^{(t)}\Lambda_{g_{2},1,j_{2}}\ldots P^{(t)}\Lambda_{g_{n},1,j_{n}}M^{(t)}_{u_{1,j_{1}}}M^{(t)}_{u_{2,j_{2}}}\ldots M^{(t)}_{u_{n,j_{n}}}P^{(t)}

(7.8)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\Theta^{(t)}_{f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}}.

Writing the operator above in integral form, for any
h\in L_{a,t}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})
,

\displaystyle\Theta^{(t)}_{f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}}h(\xi)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2n}}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Phi_{n,1}^{(t)}(|\varphi_{z_{i}}(w_{i})|^{2})\bigg]\cdot\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\langle Q_{z_{i}}\bar{\partial}g_{i}(w_{i}),z_{i}-w_{i}\rangle\bigg]\cdot\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}V_{1}(z_{i},w_{i})\bigg]

\displaystyle\cdot\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\langle Q_{\bar{w}_{n}}\partial f_{i}(w_{n}),\overline{z_{i}-w_{i}}\rangle\bigg]h(w_{n})

\displaystyle\cdot K^{(t)}_{z_{1}}(\xi)K^{(t)}_{w_{1}}(z_{1})\ldots K^{(t)}_{z_{n}}(w_{n-1})K^{(t)}_{w_{n}}(z_{n})\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(w_{n})\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{n})\ldots\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(w_{1})\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z_{1}).

We claim that
\Theta^{(t)}_{f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}}
vanishes after antisymmetrization over the
f
-symbols. To show this property, it suffices to show that

\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)\prod_{i=1}^{n}\langle Q_{\bar{w}_{n}}\partial f_{\tau_{i}}(w_{n}),\overline{z_{i}-w_{i}}\rangle=0.

(7.9)

Each
\langle Q_{\bar{w}_{n}}\partial f_{\tau_{i}}(w_{n}),\overline{z_{i}-w_{i}}\rangle
is independent of the choice of coordinates. Thus we may assume without loss of generality that
w_{n}=(r,0,\ldots,0)
. In this case, we have the expression

\langle Q_{\bar{w}_{n}}\partial f_{\tau_{i}}(w_{n}),\overline{z_{i}-w_{i}}\rangle=\sum_{j=2}^{n}(z_{i,j}-w_{i,j})\partial_{j}f_{\tau_{i}}(w_{n}).

Then the above equals

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=2}^{n}(z_{1,j_{1}}-w_{1,j_{1}})\ldots(z_{n,j_{n}}-w_{n,j_{n}})\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)\partial_{j_{1}}f_{\tau_{1}}(w_{n})\partial_{j_{2}}f_{\tau_{2}}(w_{n})\ldots\partial_{j_{n}}f_{\tau_{n}}(w_{n}).

Since
j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}
takes value in
\{2,\ldots,n\}
, at least two indices are equal. This implies that

\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)\partial_{j_{1}}f_{\tau_{1}}(w_{n})\partial_{j_{2}}f_{\tau_{2}}(w_{n})\ldots\partial_{j_{n}}f_{\tau_{n}}(w_{n})=0.

Therefore (
7.9
) holds. We conclude from this fact that the anti-symmetric sum

\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)\Theta^{(t)}_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},\ldots,f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n}}

equals zero, and complete the proof of

[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]^{\mathrm{odd}}_{t}\in\mathcal{S}^{1}.

Part 2:
We prove

\lim_{t\to\infty}\mathrm{Tr}[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]^{\mathrm{odd}}_{t}=\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\partial f_{1}\wedge\bar{\partial}g_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\partial f_{n}\wedge\bar{\partial}g_{n}.

For this part, we assume that
t
is large enough.
We use the quantization formula at
k=1
. By Theorem
6.1
, Remark
6.4
and Corollary
6.7
, for
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
, we have the following decomposition

\sigma_{t}(f,g)=R^{(t)}_{f,g,1}=c_{1,t}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f,g)}+R^{(t)}_{f,g,2}.

(7.10)

The following hold.

1.

c_{1,t}=nt^{-1}+O(t^{-2}),

2.

C_{1}(f,g)=C_{N}(f,g)+C_{T}(f,g),

where
C_{N}(f,g)
(
C_{T}(f,g)
) denotes the part involving complex normal (tangential) derivatives of
f,g
,
and

T^{(t)}_{C_{N}(f,g)}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},\forall p>\frac{n}{2},\text{ with }\|T^{(t)}_{C_{N}(f,g)}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{\frac{n}{p}},

T^{(t)}_{C_{T}(f,g)}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},\forall p>n,\text{ with }\|T^{(t)}_{C_{T}(f,g)}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{\frac{n}{p}}.

3.

R^{(t)}_{f,g,1}=\sum_{a,b=0,1}R^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,1},\quad R^{(t)}_{f,g,2}=\sum_{a,b=0,1,2}R^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,2},

where
a,b
denote the order of derivatives on
f,g
in the complex normal directions, and for large
t
,

R^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,i}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},\forall p>\frac{n}{1+\frac{a+b}{2}},\quad i=1,2,

\|R^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,i}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{-i+\frac{n}{p}}.

The above will be the main tool for Part 2 and will be repeatedly used without reference. We will prove Part 2 by establishing the following properties.

\displaystyle[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]_{t}^{\mathrm{odd}}

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1})}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2})}\ldots T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n})},

(7.11)

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1})}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2})}\ldots T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n})}

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1})C_{1}(f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2})C_{1}(f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n})},

(7.12)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\bigg[c_{1,t}^{n}\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1})C_{1}(f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2})C_{1}(f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n})}\bigg]

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\partial f_{1}\wedge\bar{\partial}g_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\partial f_{n}\wedge\bar{\partial}g_{n},t\to\infty.

(7.13)

Proof of (
7.1
).

By (
7.10
), we compute the product of semi-commutators

\displaystyle\sigma_{t}(f_{1},g_{1})\sigma_{t}(f_{2},g_{2})\ldots\sigma_{t}(f_{n},g_{n})-c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{1},g_{1})}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{2},g_{2})}\ldots T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{n},g_{n})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R^{(t)}_{f_{1},g_{1},1}R^{(t)}_{f_{2},g_{2},1}\ldots R^{(t)}_{f_{n},g_{n},1}-\bigg(R^{(1)}_{f_{1},g_{1},1}-R^{(t)}_{f_{1},g_{1},2}\bigg)\bigg(R^{(1)}_{f_{2},g_{2},1}-R^{(t)}_{f_{2},g_{2},2}\bigg)\ldots\bigg(R^{(1)}_{f_{n},g_{n},1}-R^{(t)}_{f_{n},g_{n},2}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{(i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{n})\in X}\pm R^{(t)}_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}R^{(t)}_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}\ldots R^{(t)}_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}},

where

X=\{(i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{n}):i_{j}=1,2,\text{ and at least one }i_{j}=2\}.

Therefore

[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]_{t}^{\mathrm{odd}}-c_{1,t}^{n}\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1})}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2})}\ldots T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n})}

is a linear combination of operators of the form

\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)R^{(t)}_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},i_{1}}R^{(t)}_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2},i_{2}}\ldots R^{(t)}_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n},i_{n}},

where
(i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{n})\in X
. For (
7.1
), it suffices to prove

\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)R^{(t)}_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},i_{1}}R^{(t)}_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2},i_{2}}\ldots R^{(t)}_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n},i_{n}}\sim_{a}0,\quad\forall(i_{1},\ldots,i_{n})\in X.

(7.14)

We show the case when
(i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{n})=(2,1,\ldots,1)
, i.e.,

\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)R^{(t)}_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},2}R^{(t)}_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2},1}\ldots R^{(t)}_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n},1}\sim_{a}0.

First, each
R^{(t)}_{f,g,i}
decomposes into the sum of
R^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,i}
. The operator
R^{(t)0,0}_{f,g,i}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},\forall p>n,
and

\|R^{(t)0,0}_{f,g,1}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim t^{-1+\frac{n}{p}},\quad\|R^{(t)0,0}_{f,g,2}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim t^{-2+\frac{n}{p}}.

If
(a,b)\neq(0,0)
then the operator belongs to
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for some
p<n
, with asymptotic Schatten-norm estimates

\|R^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,1}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim t^{-1+\frac{n}{p}},\quad\|R^{(t)a,b}_{f,g,2}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim t^{-2+\frac{n}{p}}.

Therefore for each
\tau\in S_{n},
we have the following equation

R^{(t)}_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},2}R^{(t)}_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2},1}\ldots R^{(t)}_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n},1}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{1},b_{1}=0,1,2\\
a_{2},b_{2},\ldots,a_{n},b_{n}=0,1\end{subarray}}R^{(t)a_{1},b_{1}}_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},2}R^{(t)a_{2},b_{2}}_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2},1}\ldots R^{(t)a_{n},b_{n}}_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n},1}.

Suppose that there is some
(a_{k},b_{k})\neq(0,0)
. Then we can choose
1\leq p_{1},\ldots,p_{n}<\infty
such that
\sum_{j}\frac{1}{p_{j}}=1
, and
R^{(t)a_{j},b_{j}}_{f_{\tau_{j}},g_{j},i_{j}}\in\mathcal{S}^{p_{i}},\ i=1,\ldots,n
. So

R^{(t)a_{1},b_{1}}_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},2}R^{(t)a_{2},b_{2}}_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2},1}\ldots R^{(t)a_{n},b_{n}}_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n},1}\in\mathcal{S}^{1},

with trace norm

\lesssim t^{-2+\frac{n}{p_{1}}}\cdot t^{-1+\frac{n}{p_{2}}}\cdot\ldots\cdot t^{-1+\frac{n}{p_{n}}}=t^{-1}.

It follows that
R^{(t)a_{1},b_{1}}_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},2}R^{(t)a_{2},b_{2}}_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2},1}\ldots R^{(t)a_{n},b_{n}}_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n},1}\sim_{a}0
. And we conclude

R^{(t)}_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},2}R^{(t)}_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2},1}\ldots R^{(t)}_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n},1}\sim_{a}R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},2}R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2},1}\ldots R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n},1}.

(7.15)

Next, we show

\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},2}R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2},1}\ldots R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n},1}\sim_{a}0.

(7.16)

The proof is an almost verbatim repetition of the proof of (
7.6
). Tracing back the definition, for
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
and
h\in L_{a,t}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})
, we compute
R_{f,g,2}^{(t)0,0}
,

\displaystyle R_{f,g,2}^{(t)0,0}h(\xi)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}\Phi_{n,2}^{(t)}(|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2})V_{2}(z,w)\langle Q_{\bar{z}}\partial f(z),\overline{z-w}\rangle\langle Q_{z}\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle

(7.17)

\displaystyle~~~~~\cdot h(w)K^{(t)}_{z}(\xi)K^{(t)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z),

with

\displaystyle V_{2}(z,w)=

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i_{2},j_{2}=1}^{n}I^{e_{i_{2}},e_{j_{2}}}(z-w)\partial_{z_{i_{2}}}\bar{\partial}_{w_{j_{2}}}(1-\langle z,w\rangle)^{2}}{(1-\langle w,z\rangle)^{2}}

(7.18)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\frac{1-\langle z,w\rangle}{(1-\langle w,z\rangle)^{2}}\cdot C(z,w)+\frac{B_{1}(z,w)B_{2}(z,w)}{(1-\langle w,z\rangle)^{2}},

(7.19)

where
C(z,w),B_{1}(z,w),B_{2}(z,w)
are as in the proof of Lemma
6.11
. By the estimates in the proof, we get

|V_{2}(z,w)|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2}.

(7.20)

Similar as in the proof of (
7.6
), we write

R^{(t)0,0}_{f,g,2}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}P^{(t)}M^{(t)}_{[Q_{z}\partial f]_{j}}\Lambda_{g,2,j},

where

\Lambda_{g,2,j}h(z)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\Phi_{n,2}^{(t)}(|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2})F_{\Lambda_{g,2,j}}(z,w)h(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(w),

with

F_{\Lambda_{g,2,j}}(z,w)=V_{2}(z,w)(z_{j}-w_{j})\langle Q_{z}\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle K^{(t)}_{w}(z).

By (
7.20
) and Lemma
2.2
, we have the following estimate

\big|F_{\Lambda_{g,2,j}}(z,w)\big|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2}\frac{1}{|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{n+1+t-1}}.

Again, by Theorem
4.6
and Corollary
4.7
, for any Lipschitz function
u
on
\mathbb{B}_{n}
, we have the following properties.

•

Both
P^{(t)}\Lambda_{g,2,j}
and
\Lambda_{g,2,j}P^{(t)}
are in
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>n
, with Schatten-
p
norm
\lesssim_{p}t^{-2+\frac{n}{p}}
.

•

Both
P^{(t)}[\Lambda_{g,2,j},M^{(t)}_{u}]
and
[\Lambda_{g,2,j},M^{(t)}_{u}]P^{(t)}
are in
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>\frac{2n}{3}
, with Schatten-
p
norm
\lesssim_{p}t^{-2+\frac{n}{p}}
.

•

For any Lipschitz function
u
,
[P^{(t)},M_{u}^{(t)}]=[P^{(t)},M_{u}^{(t)}]P^{(t)}-P^{(t)}[P^{(t)},M_{u}^{(t)}]\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>2n
, with
\|[P^{(t)},M_{u}^{(t)}]\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{\frac{n}{p}}
.

Then as in the proof of (
7.1
), we compute the product of
R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{\tau_{1}},f_{1},2}
and
R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{\tau_{i}},g_{i},1}
(
i=2,\cdots,n
)

\displaystyle R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},2}R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2},1}\ldots R^{(t)0,0}_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n},1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}P^{(t)}M^{(t)}_{u_{\tau_{1},j_{1}}}\Lambda_{g_{1},2,j_{1}}P^{(t)}M^{(t)}_{u_{\tau_{2},j_{2}}}\Lambda_{g_{2},1,j_{2}}\ldots P^{(t)}M^{(t)}_{u_{\tau_{n},j_{n}}}\Lambda_{g_{n},1,j_{n}}P^{(t)}

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}P^{(t)}\Lambda_{g_{1},2,j_{1}}P^{(t)}\Lambda_{g_{2},1,j_{2}}\ldots P^{(t)}\Lambda_{g_{n},1,j_{n}}M^{(t)}_{u_{\tau_{1},j_{1}}}M^{(t)}_{u_{\tau_{2},j_{2}}}\ldots M^{(t)}_{u_{\tau_{n},j_{n}}}P^{(t)}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\Theta^{{}^{\prime}(t)}_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},\ldots,f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n}}.

Again, by (
7.9
), we conclude with the following equation

\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)\Theta^{{}^{\prime}(t)}_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},\ldots,f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n}}=0.

This proves (
7.16
). And together with (
7.15
), it proves (
7.14
) for
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{n})=(2,1,\ldots,1)
. The proof for general
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{n})\in X
is an almost verbatim repetition of the above. This finishes the proof of (
7.1
).

Proof of (
7.1
).

Denote

\phi_{i}(z)=(1-|z|^{2})^{i},\quad i=1,2,\ldots.

Suppose
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
.
For
z\neq 0
, let
\mathbf{e}_{z}=\{e_{z,1},e_{z,2},\ldots,e_{z,n}\}
be an orthonormal basis of
\mathbb{C}^{n}
such that
e_{z,1}=\frac{z}{|z|}
. Let
\varphi:\mathbb{C}^{n}\to[0,1]
be a smooth function that equals
1
inside
\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{B}_{n}
and vanishes outside
\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}_{n}
. Under the basis
\mathbf{e}_{z}
, let

D_{f,g,1}(z)=-\frac{1}{n}\bigg[\sum_{i=2}^{n}\partial_{i}f(z)\bar{\partial}_{i}g(z)\bigg](1-\varphi(z)),

(7.21)

and

D_{f,g,2}(z)=-\frac{1}{n}\partial_{1}g(z)\bar{\partial}_{1}g(z)(1-\varphi(z))+\frac{C_{1}(f,g)(z)\varphi(z)}{(1-|z|^{2})^{2}}.

(7.22)

Then by Remark
6.4
and direct computation, we have the following decomposition

C_{1}(f,g)=\phi_{1}D_{f,g,1}+\phi_{2}D_{f,g,2}.

By Remark
6.5
,
D_{f,g,1},D_{f,g,2}\in\mathscr{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. With the decomposition we have the following formula

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{1},g_{1})}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{2},g_{2})}\ldots T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{n},g_{n})}-T^{(t)}_{c_{1,t}^{n}C_{1}(f_{1},g_{1})C_{1}(f_{2},g_{2})\ldots C_{1}(f_{n},g_{n})}

(7.23)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}\sum_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}=1,2}\bigg[T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{2}}D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{n}}D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}-T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}\phi_{i_{2}}D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}\ldots\phi_{i_{n}}D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}\bigg].

By Theorem
6.1
, Corollary
6.8
and Lemma
4.8
for
t
large enough,
i,j=1,2,\ldots
, and
u,v\in\mathscr{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
, we obtain the following estimates.

T^{(t)}_{u}T^{(t)}_{v}-T^{(t)}_{uv}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},~\forall p>n,\quad\text{ and }\|T^{(t)}_{u}T^{(t)}_{v}-T^{(t)}_{uv}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{-1+\frac{n}{p}},

T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}}T^{(t)}_{u}-T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}u}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},~\forall p>\frac{n}{i+\frac{1}{2}},\quad\text{ and }\|T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}}T^{(t)}_{u}-T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}u}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{-1+\frac{n}{p}},

T^{(t)}_{u}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}}-T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}u}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},~\forall p>\frac{n}{i+\frac{1}{2}},\quad\text{ and }\|T^{(t)}_{u}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}}-T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}u}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{-1+\frac{n}{p}},

and

T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j}}-T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}\phi_{j}}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},~\forall p>\frac{n}{i+j},\quad\text{ and }\|T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j}}-T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}\phi_{j}}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{-1+\frac{n}{p}},

T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}u}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},~\forall p>\frac{n}{i},\quad\text{ and }\|T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}u}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{\frac{n}{p}},

and

c_{1,t}\approx t^{-1}.

Therefore by the above estimates, we compute the product of
T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{j}}D_{f_{j},g_{j},i_{j}}}
.

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{2}}D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{n}}D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{2}}D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{n}}D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-c_{1,t}^{n}\big(T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}}-T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}}\big)T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{2}}D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{n}}D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{2}}D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{n}}D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}

\displaystyle\ldots

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{2}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{n}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{2}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{n}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}}\big[T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}},T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{2}}}\big]T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{n}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{2}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{n}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}

\displaystyle\ldots

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{n}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{n}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{n}}}\bigg(T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}}-T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}}\bigg)T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{3}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{n}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{3}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}

\displaystyle\ldots

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{n}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}\ldots D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}.

If some
i_{k}=2
in the above, we continue the above computation as follows,

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}\phi_{i_{2}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{3}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{n}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}\ldots D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}

\displaystyle\ldots

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}\phi_{i_{2}}\ldots\phi_{i_{n}}}T^{(t)}_{D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}\ldots D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}\phi_{i_{2}}\ldots\phi_{i_{n}}D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}\ldots D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}.

This proves that if there is some
i_{k}=2
, then we have the following equation

c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{2}}D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{n}}D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}\sim_{a}c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i_{1}}\phi_{i_{2}}\ldots\phi_{i_{n}}D_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}D_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}\ldots D_{f_{n},g_{n},i_{n}}}.

(7.24)

If all
i_{k}=1
, in the steps right above (
7.24
), the difference operator may not belong to the trace class. We need to take the anti-symmetrization into consideration. Similar as in the proof of (
7.9
), the odd anti-symmetrization is over
n
symbols, but
Q_{z}\partial f
has only
n-1
entries under the basis
\mathbf{e}_{z}
. Thus we have the identities

\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)D_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},1}D_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2},1}\ldots D_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n},1}=0,

(7.25)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{1}D_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},1}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{1}D_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2},1}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{1}D_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n},1}}

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}\bigg[T^{(t)}_{\phi_{1}}\bigg]^{n}T^{(t)}_{\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)D_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},1}D_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2},1}\ldots D_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n},1}}

(7.26)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

Also by (
7.25
), we have the following equation

\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)c_{1,t}^{n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{1}D_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},1}\phi_{1}D_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2},1}\ldots\phi_{1}D_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n},1}}=T^{(t)}_{\phi_{1}^{n}\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)D_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},1}D_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2},1}\ldots D_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n},1}}=0.

(7.27)

Altogether, (
7.23
), (
7.24
), (
7.1
) and (
7.27
) imply (
7.1
).

Proof of (
7.1
).

Denote

F(z)=\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)C_{1}(f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1})\ldots C_{1}(f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n}).

Under the basis
\mathbf{e}_{z}
, we compute the following volume form

\displaystyle(-1)^{n}n^{n}(1-|z|^{2})^{-n-1}F\mathrm{d}z_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\mathrm{d}z_{n}\wedge\mathrm{d}\bar{z}_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\mathrm{d}\bar{z}_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau,\varsigma\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)\partial_{\varsigma(1)}f_{\tau_{1}}\bar{\partial}_{\varsigma(1)}g_{1}\ldots\partial_{\varsigma(n)}f_{\tau_{n}}\bar{\partial}_{\varsigma(n)}g_{n}\mathrm{d}z_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\mathrm{d}z_{n}\wedge\mathrm{d}\bar{z}_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\mathrm{d}\bar{z}_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau,\varsigma\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)\big(\partial_{\varsigma(1)}f_{\tau_{1}}\mathrm{d}z_{\varsigma(1)}\big)\wedge\ldots\wedge\big(\partial_{\varsigma(n)}f_{\tau_{n}}\mathrm{d}z_{\varsigma(n)}\big)\wedge\big(\bar{\partial}_{\varsigma(1)}g_{1}\mathrm{d}\bar{z}_{\varsigma(1)}\big)\wedge\ldots\wedge\big(\bar{\partial}_{\varsigma(n)}g_{n}\mathrm{d}\bar{z}_{\varsigma(n)}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau,\varsigma\in S_{n}}\big(\partial_{\varsigma\tau^{-1}(1)}f_{1}\mathrm{d}z_{\varsigma\tau^{-1}(1)}\big)\wedge\ldots\wedge\big(\partial_{\varsigma\tau^{-1}(n)}f_{n}\mathrm{d}z_{\varsigma\tau^{-1}(n)}\big)\wedge\big(\bar{\partial}_{\varsigma(1)}g_{1}\mathrm{d}\bar{z}_{\varsigma(1)}\big)\wedge\ldots\wedge\big(\bar{\partial}_{\varsigma(n)}g_{n}\mathrm{d}\bar{z}_{\varsigma(n)}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\iota,\varsigma\in S_{n}}\big(\partial_{\iota(1)}f_{1}\mathrm{d}z_{\iota(1)}\big)\wedge\ldots\wedge\big(\partial_{\iota(n)}f_{n}\mathrm{d}z_{\iota(n)}\big)\wedge\big(\bar{\partial}_{\varsigma(1)}g_{1}\mathrm{d}\bar{z}_{\varsigma(1)}\big)\wedge\ldots\wedge\big(\bar{\partial}_{\varsigma(n)}g_{n}\mathrm{d}\bar{z}_{\varsigma(n)}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial f_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\partial f_{n}\wedge\bar{\partial}g_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\bar{\partial}g_{n}.

Therefore, we obtain the following formula of the trace

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}T^{(t)}_{\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)c_{1,t}^{n}C_{1}(f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1})C_{1}(f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2})C_{1}(f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{1,t}^{n}\mathrm{Tr}T^{(t)}_{F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(n-1)!c_{1,t}^{n}}{\pi^{n}B(n,t+1)}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\frac{F(z)}{(1-|z|^{2})^{n+1}}\mathrm{d}m(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(n-1)!c_{1,t}^{n}}{\pi^{n}B(n,t+1)}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n^{n}}\frac{1}{(-2i)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\partial f_{1}\wedge\bar{\partial}g_{1}\wedge\ldots\wedge\partial f_{n}\wedge\bar{\partial}g_{n}.

Since

c_{1,t}=nt^{-1}+O(t^{-2}),\quad B(n,t+1)=(n-1)!t^{-n}+O(t^{-n-1}),

we have

\frac{c_{1,t}^{n}}{B(n,t+1)}\to\frac{n^{n}}{(n-1)!},\quad t\to\infty.

Simplifying the above gives (
7.1
).

In summary, we have proved (
7.1
), (
7.1
) and (
7.1
). Altogether they finish Part 2. we have completed the proof of Theorem
7.3
for
t>-1
.

7.2
Proof of Theorem
7.3
:
t=-1

To simplify notations, we write

\sigma(f,g)=\sigma_{-1}(f,g),

and

[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]^{\mathrm{odd}}=[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]^{\mathrm{odd}}_{-1},

[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]^{\mathrm{even}}=[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]^{\mathrm{even}}_{-1}.

Lemma 7.8
.

Suppose
g\in\mathscr{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. Then for
h\in H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})
,

T_{g}h(z)=g(z)h(z)-\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{-2n}\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{(1-\langle w,z\rangle)}h(w)K_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w),\quad\forall z\in\mathbb{B}_{n}.

(7.28)

Moreover, for
z\in\mathbb{S}_{n}
almost everywhere,

T_{g}h(z)=g(z)h(z)-\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,z\rangle}h(w)K_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w).

(7.29)

Proof.

Suppose
h\in\mathrm{Hol}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. For
z\in\mathbb{B}_{n}
, apply Lemma
2.12
with
\alpha=\beta=0
,
v(w)=g(w)h(w)
. Then we compute
T_{g}
as follows,

\displaystyle T_{g}h(z)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}g(w)h(w)K_{w}(z)\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma(w)}{\sigma_{2n-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d_{0,0}(z)g(z)h(z)-\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{-2n}\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bar{\partial}_{j}\big[g(w)h(w)\big]\overline{(z_{j}-w_{j})}}{1-\langle w,z\rangle}K_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g(z)h(z)-\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{-2n}\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,z\rangle}h(w)K_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w).

This proves (
7.28
) for
h\in\mathrm{Hol}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. The equation for general
h\in H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})
follows from approximation.

For
h\in\mathrm{Hol}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
,
z\in\mathbb{S}_{n}
and
\frac{1}{2}<r<1
, we compute
T_{g}h(rz)

T_{g}h(rz)=g(rz)h(rz)-\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}|\varphi_{rz}(w)|^{-2n}\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),rz-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,rz\rangle}h(w)K_{w}(rz)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w).

The first term on the right hand side tends to
g(z)h(z)
. In order to prove (
7.29
), it suffices to prove the following identity

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 1^{-}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}|\varphi_{rz}(w)|^{-2n}\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),rz-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,rz\rangle}h(w)K_{w}(rz)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w)

(7.30)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,z\rangle}h(w)K_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w).

By Lemma
2.2
, we have the following estimate

\bigg|\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),rz-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,rz\rangle}h(w)K_{w}(rz)\bigg|\lesssim\frac{|\varphi_{rz}(w)|}{|1-\langle rz,w\rangle|^{n+1/2}}.

Therefore we have the following estimate of the integrals

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}|\varphi_{rz}(w)|^{-2n}\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),rz-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,rz\rangle}h(w)K_{w}(rz)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w)-\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),rz-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,rz\rangle}h(w)K_{w}(rz)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w)\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\big(|\varphi_{rz}(w)|^{-2n}-1\big)\cdot\frac{|\varphi_{rz}(w)|}{|1-\langle rz,w\rangle|^{n+1/2}}\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}|\varphi_{rz}(w)|^{-2n+1}\big(1-|\varphi_{rz}(w)|^{2}\big)\frac{1}{|1-\langle rz,w\rangle|^{n+1/2}}\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(1-|rz|^{2})^{1/2}\to 0,\quad r\to 1^{-}.

Here the last inequality follows from Lemma
2.3
(3). Therefore, we have the following limit computation,

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 1^{-}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}|\varphi_{rz}(w)|^{-2n}\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),rz-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,rz\rangle}h(w)K_{w}(rz)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w)

(7.31)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{r\to 1^{-}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),rz-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,rz\rangle}h(w)K_{w}(rz)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w).

For
\frac{1}{2}<r<1
and
|a|<1
, it is easy to verify that

\frac{1}{|1-ra|}\leq\frac{2}{|1-a|}.

So we have the following bounds

\bigg|\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),rz-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,rz\rangle}h(w)K_{w}(rz)\bigg|\lesssim\frac{|\varphi_{rz}(w)|}{|1-\langle rz,w\rangle|^{n+1/2}}\lesssim\frac{1}{|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{n+1/2}}.

Thus by the Dominated Convergence Theorem, we arrive at the following equation

\lim_{r\to 1^{-}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),rz-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,rz\rangle}h(w)K_{w}(rz)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,z\rangle}h(w)K_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w).

(7.32)

Combining (
7.31
) and (
7.32
) gives (
7.30
). Thus (
7.29
) holds pointwise for
h\in\mathrm{Hol}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. The general case follows from approximation. This completes the proof of Lemma
7.8
.
∎

Lemma 7.9
.

Suppose
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. Then

\sigma(f,g)=R_{f,g,1},

where the operator
R_{f,g,1}
is defined as follows.

R_{f,g,1}h(\xi)=-\frac{1}{n^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{-2n}\frac{\langle\partial f(z),\overline{z-w}\rangle\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{(1-\langle w,z\rangle)^{2}}K_{w}(z)K_{z}(\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(z).

Proof.

Suppose
h\in\mathrm{Hol}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. By Lemma
7.8
, for
z\in\mathbb{S}_{n}
, we compute
T_{g}h

T_{g}h(z)=g(z)h(z)-\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,z\rangle}h(w)K_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w).

Therefore for
\xi\in\mathbb{B}_{n}
, we have the following computation of
T_{f}T_{g}
,

\displaystyle T_{f}T_{g}h(\xi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\bigg\{g(z)h(z)-\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,z\rangle}h(w)K_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w)\bigg\}f(z)K_{z}(\xi)\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma(z)}{\sigma_{2n-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}g(z)f(z)h(z)K_{z}(\xi)\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma(z)}{\sigma_{2n-1}}-\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,z\rangle}h(w)K_{w}(z)f(z)K_{z}(\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w)\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma(z)}{\sigma_{2n-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{fg}h(\xi)-\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\frac{\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,z\rangle}h(w)K_{w}(z)f(z)K_{z}(\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w)\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma(z)}{\sigma_{2n-1}}.

Since
f,g,h
are bounded, using Lemma
2.3
we see that the double integral on the right converges absolutely. By Fubini’s Theorem, we have

\sigma(f,g)h(\xi)=-\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\bigg\{\int_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\frac{f(z)\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,z\rangle}K_{w}(z)K_{z}(\xi)\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma(z)}{\sigma_{2n-1}}\bigg\}h(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w).

For fixed
w,\xi\in\mathbb{B}_{n}
, applying (
2.15
) with
\alpha=\beta=0
,
v(z)=\frac{f(z)\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,z\rangle}K_{z}(\xi)
gives

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\frac{f(z)\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,z\rangle}K_{w}(z)K_{z}(\xi)\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma(z)}{\sigma_{2n-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{-2n}\frac{\langle\partial f(z),\overline{z-w}\rangle\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{(1-\langle w,z\rangle)^{2}}K_{w}(z)K_{z}(\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(z).

Therefore we get the following formula for
\sigma(f,g)

\displaystyle\sigma(f,g)h(\xi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\bigg\{\frac{1}{n}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{-2n}\frac{\langle\partial f(z),\overline{z-w}\rangle\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{(1-\langle w,z\rangle)^{2}}K_{w}(z)K_{z}(\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(z)\bigg\}h(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}^{2}}|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{-2n}\frac{\langle\partial f(z),\overline{z-w}\rangle\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{(1-\langle w,z\rangle)^{2}}K_{w}(z)K_{z}(\xi)h(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(z).

This completes the proof of Lemma
7.9
.
∎

Definition 7.10
.

Suppose
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
and
h\in L^{2}(\lambda_{0})
.

1.

Define

Ph(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}h(z)K_{z}(\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(z),

and

\Gamma_{f,g}h(z)=-\frac{1}{n^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{-2n}\frac{\langle\partial f(z),\overline{z-w}\rangle\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{(1-\langle w,z\rangle)^{2}}K_{w}(z)h(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w).

Then

R_{f,g,1}=P\Gamma_{f,g}.

2.

Define

A^{0}=\langle Q_{\bar{z}}\partial f(z),\overline{(z-w)}\rangle,\quad A^{1}=\langle P_{\bar{z}}\partial f(z),\overline{(z-w)}\rangle,

B^{0}=\langle Q_{z}\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle,\quad B^{1}=\langle P_{z}\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle,

and

C^{a,b}(z,w)=A^{a}B^{b},\quad a,b=0,1.

Then

|C^{a,b}(z,w)|\lesssim|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{1+\frac{a+b}{2}},

(7.33)

and

\sum_{a,b=0,1}C^{a,b}(z,w)=\langle\partial f(z),\overline{z-w}\rangle\langle\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle.

3.

Define

\Gamma^{a,b}_{f,g}h(z)=-\frac{1}{n^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{-2n}\frac{C^{a,b}(z,w)}{(1-\langle w,z\rangle)^{2}}K_{w}(z)h(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w).

Then

\Gamma_{f,g}=\sum_{a,b=0,1}\Gamma^{a,b}_{f,g}.

4.

Define for
j=1,\ldots,n
,

\Lambda_{g,j}h(z)=-\frac{1}{n^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{-2n}(z_{j}-w_{j})\frac{\langle Q_{z}\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{(1-\langle w,z\rangle)^{2}}K_{w}(z)h(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w).

Then

\Gamma^{0,0}_{f,g}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}M_{[Q_{\bar{z}}\partial f]_{j}}\Lambda_{g,j}.

Lemma 7.11
.

Suppose
n\geq 2
,
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
,
u\in\mathscr{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
, and
a,b=0,1
. Consider
P,\Gamma^{a,b}_{f,g},\Lambda_{g,j}
as operators on
L^{2}(\lambda_{0})
. Then the following hold.

(1)

[P^{(0)},M_{u}]\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>2n
.

(2)

P\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>n
.

(3)

[P,M_{u}]\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>\frac{2n}{3}
.

(4)

If
(a,b)\neq(0,0)
, then
\Gamma^{a,b}_{f,g}P^{(0)}\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
for
p
large enough, and
\Gamma^{a,b}_{f,g}P\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
for some
p<n
.

(5)

For each
j
,
\Lambda_{g,j}
is bounded.

(6)

For each
j
,
[\Lambda_{g,j},M_{u}]P^{(0)}\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>2n
.

(7)

For each
j
,
[\Lambda_{g,j},M_{u}]P\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>\frac{2n}{3}
.

Proof.

By the integral formula of
P
and the Bergman projection
P^{(0)}
, it is easy to see that (2) follows from Corollary
4.2
, and (1) follows from Corollary
4.3
. If
n\geq 3
then (3) also follows from Corollary
4.3
. At
n=2
, notice that
P
is self-adjoint and has range in
L_{a,0}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})
. Thus we compute the commutator
[P,M_{u}]

[P,M_{u}]=P^{(0)}[P,M_{u}]+(1-P^{(0)})[P,M_{u}]=\bigg([M_{\bar{u}},P]P^{(0)}\bigg)^{*}-H^{(0)}_{u}P.

By (2) and Corollary
4.4
, the second term on the right belongs to
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>\frac{2n}{3}
. For any
h\in\mathrm{Hol}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
, apply (
2.11
) with
t=0
,
\alpha=\beta=0
,
\phi=1
, and
v(w)=\big(\bar{u}(z)-\bar{u}(w)\big)h(w)(1-\langle z,w\rangle)
. Then we get the following expression

\displaystyle[M_{\bar{u}},P]h(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\big(\bar{u}(z)-\bar{u}(w)\big)h(w)K_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\mathcal{G}^{(0)}_{n}1(|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2})\frac{(1-|w|^{2})\langle\bar{\partial}v(w),z-w\rangle}{1-\langle w,z\rangle}K^{(0)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w)

(7.34)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\mathcal{G}^{(0)}_{n}1(|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2})\bigg((1-\langle z,w\rangle)\langle\bar{\partial}\bar{u}(w),z-w\rangle+\big(\bar{u}(z)-\bar{u}(w)\big)\langle z,z-w\rangle\bigg)

\displaystyle~~~~~~~~\cdot\frac{1-|w|^{2}}{1-\langle w,z\rangle}h(w)K^{(0)}_{w}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w).

Take

F(z,w)=\frac{2}{n+1}\bigg((1-\langle z,w\rangle)\langle\bar{\partial}\bar{u}(w),z-w\rangle+\big(\bar{u}(z)-\bar{u}(w)\big)\langle z,z-w\rangle\bigg)\frac{1}{1-\langle w,z\rangle}K^{(0)}_{w}(z).

Substituting
F
into the integral on the right hand side of Equation (
7.2
) gives the following formula

[M_{\bar{u}},P]h(z)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\mathcal{G}_{n}^{(0)}1(|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2})F(z,w)h(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{1}(w).

By Lemma
5.8
, we have the following estimate

|\mathcal{G}^{(0)}_{n}1(s)|\lesssim s^{-n}.

By Lemma
2.2
and the fact that
\bar{u}\in\mathscr{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
, we obtain the following bound

\big|F(z,w)\big|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)|\frac{1}{|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{n+1/2}}.

For any
\epsilon>0
, split the map as follows.

[M_{\bar{u}},P]P^{(0)}:L_{a,0}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})\xrightarrow{E_{0,3-\epsilon}}L_{a,3-\epsilon}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})\xrightarrow{T}L^{2}(\lambda_{0}),

where
Th(z)
is defined as in the last line of Equation (
7.2
). Then by the estimates above and Lemma
3.1
,
T
is bounded. Thus by Lemma
3.4
,
[M_{\bar{u}},P]P^{(0)}
is in
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>\frac{2n}{3}
. This proves (3).

Let

F_{\Gamma^{a,b}}(z,w)=-\frac{1}{n^{2}}\frac{C^{a,b}(z,w)}{(1-\langle w,z\rangle)^{2}}K_{w}(z),\quad z,w\in\mathbb{B}_{n},

and

F_{\Lambda_{g,j}}(z,w)=-\frac{1}{n^{2}}(z_{j}-w_{j})\frac{\langle Q_{z}\bar{\partial}g(w),z-w\rangle}{(1-\langle w,z\rangle)^{2}}K_{w}(z),\quad z,w\in\mathbb{B}_{n},j=1,\ldots,n.

Then write

\Gamma_{f,g}^{a,b}h(z)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{-2n}F_{\Gamma^{a,b}}(z,w)h(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w),

and

\Lambda_{g,j}h(z)=\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{-2n}F_{\Lambda_{g,j}}(z,w)h(w)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(w).

By (
7.33
) and Lemma
2.2
, we have the following bound

\big|F_{\Gamma^{a,b}}(z,w)\big|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2}\frac{1}{|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{n+1-\frac{a+b}{2}}},

and

\big|F_{\Lambda_{g,j}}(z,w)\big|\lesssim|\varphi_{z}(w)|^{2}\frac{1}{|1-\langle z,w\rangle|^{n+1}}.

Thus by Lemma
3.1
,
\Lambda_{g,j}
is bounded. This proves (5). By Lemma
4.5
,
\Gamma_{f,g}^{a,b}P^{(0)}\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
for
p
large enough. Since
P\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>n
, and
\Gamma^{a,b}_{f,g}P=\Gamma_{f,g}^{a,b}P^{(0)}P
, we have
\Gamma^{a,b}_{f,g}P\in\mathcal{S}^{p}
for some
p<n
. This proves (4). Also, (6) follows from the above estimates and Corollary
4.7
, and (7) follows again from (6) and the equation
P=P^{(0)}P
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 7.12
.

Denote
\hat{P}
the operator from
L^{2}(\lambda_{0})
to
H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})
defined by the same integral formula as
P
. Then
\hat{P}=E_{-1,1}^{*}
.

Proof.

For any
f\in L^{2}(\lambda_{0})
,
g\in H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})
, we compute
\langle\hat{P}f,g\rangle_{H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})}

\displaystyle\langle\hat{P}f,g\rangle_{H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})}=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}f(z)K_{z}(\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(z)\overline{g(\xi)}\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma(\xi)}{\sigma_{2n-1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\overline{g(\xi)}K_{z}(\xi)\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma(\xi)}{\sigma_{2n-1}}f(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\overline{g(z)}f(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{0}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle f,E_{-1,1}g\rangle_{L^{2}(\lambda_{0})}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7.3
(
t=-1
).

For any
\tau\in S_{n}
, split the map

\sigma(f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1})\ldots\sigma(f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n})=P\Gamma_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1}}P\Gamma_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2}}\ldots P\Gamma_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n}}

as follows.

H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})\xrightarrow{E_{-1,1}}L_{a,0}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})\xrightarrow{\Gamma_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1}}P\Gamma_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2}}\ldots P\Gamma_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n}}P^{(0)}}L^{2}(\lambda_{0})\xrightarrow{\hat{P}}H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n}).

(7.35)

By Lemma
3.4
and Lemma
7.12
, the operators on the two ends of (
7.35
) are in
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for any
p>2n
. Thus it suffices to show that

\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)\Gamma_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1}}P\Gamma_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2}}\ldots P\Gamma_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n}}P^{(0)}

defines an operator in
\mathcal{S}^{p}(L^{2}(\lambda_{0}))
for some
p<\frac{n}{n-1}
.

Notation 7.13
.

For operators
A,B
, temporarily write
A\sim_{p}B
when
A-B
is in
\mathcal{S}^{p}(L^{2}(\lambda_{0}))
for some
p<\frac{n}{n-1}
.

Denote

u_{i,j}(z)=[Q_{\bar{z}}\partial f_{i}(z)]_{j}.

Then by Lemma
7.11
, we compute an element in the above sum

\displaystyle\Gamma_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1}}P\Gamma_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2}}\ldots P\Gamma_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n}}P^{(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{a,b=0,1}\Gamma^{a,b}_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1}}\bigg)P\bigg(\sum_{a,b=0,1}\Gamma^{a,b}_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2}}\bigg)\ldots P\bigg(\sum_{a,b=0,1}\Gamma^{a,b}_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n}}\bigg)P^{(0)}

\displaystyle\sim_{p}

\displaystyle\Gamma^{0,0}_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1}}P\Gamma^{0,0}_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2}}\ldots P\Gamma^{0,0}_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n}}P^{(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}M_{u_{\tau_{1},j_{1}}}\Lambda_{g_{1},j_{1}}PM_{u_{\tau_{2},j_{2}}}\Lambda_{g_{2},j_{2}}P\ldots M_{u_{\tau_{n},j_{n}}}\Lambda_{g_{n},j_{n}}P^{(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}\Lambda_{g_{1},j_{1}}M_{u_{\tau_{1},j_{1}}}PM_{u_{\tau_{2},j_{2}}}\Lambda_{g_{2},j_{2}}P\ldots M_{u_{\tau_{n},j_{n}}}\Lambda_{g_{n},j_{n}}P^{(0)}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}[M_{u_{\tau_{1},j_{1}}},\Lambda_{g_{1},j_{1}}]PM_{u_{\tau_{2},j_{2}}}\Lambda_{g_{2},j_{2}}P\ldots M_{u_{\tau_{n},j_{n}}}\Lambda_{g_{n},j_{n}}P^{(0)}.

Each operator in the second term contains
n-2
copies of
P
and one
[M_{u_{\tau_{1},j_{1}}},\Lambda_{g_{1},j_{1}}]P
. By Lemma
7.11
(2) and (7), it belongs to
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for some
p<\frac{n}{n-1}
. Thus we compute the following sum,

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}M_{u_{\tau_{1},j_{1}}}\Lambda_{g_{1},j_{1}}PM_{u_{\tau_{2},j_{2}}}\Lambda_{g_{2},j_{2}}P\ldots M_{u_{\tau_{n},j_{n}}}\Lambda_{g_{n},j_{n}}P^{(0)}

\displaystyle\sim_{p}

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}\Lambda_{g_{1},j_{1}}M_{u_{\tau_{1},j_{1}}}PM_{u_{\tau_{2},j_{2}}}\Lambda_{g_{2},j_{2}}P\ldots M_{u_{\tau_{n},j_{n}}}\Lambda_{g_{n},j_{n}}P^{(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}\Lambda_{g_{1},j_{1}}PM_{u_{\tau_{1},j_{1}}}M_{u_{\tau_{2},j_{2}}}\Lambda_{g_{2},j_{2}}P\ldots M_{u_{\tau_{n},j_{n}}}\Lambda_{g_{n},j_{n}}P^{(0)}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}\Lambda_{g_{1},j_{1}}[M_{u_{\tau_{1},j_{1}}},P]M_{u_{\tau_{2},j_{2}}}\Lambda_{g_{2},j_{2}}P\ldots M_{u_{\tau_{n},j_{n}}}\Lambda_{g_{n},j_{n}}P^{(0)}.

By Lemma
7.11
(2) and (3), the last term belongs to
\mathcal{S}^{p}
for some
p<\frac{n}{n-1}
. Therefore we have the following equation

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}M_{u_{\tau_{1},j_{1}}}\Lambda_{g_{1},j_{1}}PM_{u_{\tau_{2},j_{2}}}\Lambda_{g_{2},j_{2}}P\ldots M_{u_{\tau_{n},j_{n}}}\Lambda_{g_{n},j_{n}}P^{(0)}

\displaystyle\sim_{p}

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}\Lambda_{g_{1},j_{1}}PM_{u_{\tau_{1},j_{1}}}M_{u_{\tau_{2},j_{2}}}\Lambda_{g_{2},j_{2}}P\ldots M_{u_{\tau_{n},j_{n}}}\Lambda_{g_{n},j_{n}}P^{(0)}.

Continuing like this, we obtain the following equation

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}M_{u_{\tau_{1},j_{1}}}\Lambda_{g_{1},j_{1}}PM_{u_{\tau_{2},j_{2}}}\Lambda_{g_{2},j_{2}}P\ldots M_{u_{\tau_{n},j_{n}}}\Lambda_{g_{n},j_{n}}P^{(0)}

\displaystyle\sim_{p}

\displaystyle\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{n}=1}^{n}\Lambda_{g_{1},j_{1}}P\Lambda_{g_{2},j_{2}}P\ldots\Lambda_{g_{n},j_{n}}M_{u_{\tau_{1},j_{1}}}M_{u_{\tau_{2},j_{2}}}\ldots M_{u_{\tau_{n},j_{n}}}P^{(0)}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\Theta_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},\ldots,f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n}}.

As in the proof of Theorem
7.3
, by writing
\Theta_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},\ldots,f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n}}
as an integral operator, and by (
7.9
), we can show that

\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)\Theta_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1},\ldots,f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n}}=0.

Therefore we conclude

\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)\Gamma_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1}}P\Gamma_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2}}\ldots P\Gamma_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n}}P^{(0)}\in\mathcal{S}^{p}

for some
p<\frac{n}{n-1}
. Thus by (
7.35
), we obtain

[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]^{\mathrm{odd}}=P\bigg(\sum_{\tau\in S_{n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)\Gamma_{f_{\tau_{1}},g_{1}}P\Gamma_{f_{\tau_{2}},g_{2}}\ldots P\Gamma_{f_{\tau_{n}},g_{n}}P^{(0)}\bigg)E_{-1,1}

is in the trace class. Since

[f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{n},g_{n}]^{\mathrm{odd}}=\bigg([\bar{g_{n}},\bar{f_{n}},\ldots,\bar{g_{1}},\bar{f_{1}}]^{\mathrm{even}}\bigg)^{*},

the even anti-symmetric sum is also in the trace class. Finally, as in the proof of Corollary
7.6
, we obtain

[T_{f_{1}},T_{f_{2}},\ldots,T_{f_{2n}}]=\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\tau\in S_{2n}}\mathrm{sgn}(\tau)[f_{\tau_{1}},f_{\tau_{2}},\ldots,f_{\tau_{2n}}]^{\mathrm{odd}}\in\mathcal{S}^{1}.

This completes the proof of Theorem
7.3
for
t=-1
.
∎

8
Main Theorems

In this section, we prove the main results of this article.

8.1
Helton-Howe cocycle

We are ready to prove the following main theorem.

Theorem 8.1
.

Suppose
f_{1},f_{2},\ldots,f_{2n}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
and
t\geq-1
. Then the following hold.

1.

The antisymmetric sum
[T^{(t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t)}_{f_{2n}}]
is in the trace class
\mathcal{S}^{1}
.

2.

\mathrm{Tr}[T^{(t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t)}_{f_{2n}}]=\frac{n!}{(2\pi i)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\mathrm{d}f_{1}\wedge\mathrm{d}f_{2}\wedge\ldots\wedge\mathrm{d}f_{2n},

(8.1)

which is independent of
t
.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.2
, the operator on
H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})
,

[T_{f_{1}},T_{f_{2}},\ldots,T_{f_{2n}}]-[T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{1}},T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{2n}}]

is a trace class operator of zero trace. By Corollary
7.6
, the operator

[T_{f_{1}},T_{f_{2}},\ldots,T_{f_{2n}}]

is itself in the trace class. Thus we have the following of traces

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}[T_{f_{1}},T_{f_{2}},\ldots,T_{f_{2n}}]=\mathrm{Tr}[T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{1}},T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{2n}}].

On the other hand, by Corollary
7.6
, the operator

[T^{(1)}_{f_{1}},\ldots,T^{(1)}_{f_{2n}}]

is a trace class operator on
L_{a,1}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})
. Since

[T^{(1)}_{f_{1}},\ldots,T^{(1)}_{f_{2n}}]\big|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{S}_{n})}=[T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{1}},T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{2n}}],

by Lemma
2.4
, we get the following equation of traces

\mathrm{Tr}[T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{1}},T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(1,-1)}_{f_{2n}}]=\mathrm{Tr}[T^{(1)}_{f_{1}},\ldots,T^{(1)}_{f_{2n}}].

Therefore we arrive at the following equation

\mathrm{Tr}[T_{f_{1}},T_{f_{2}},\ldots,T_{f_{2n}}]=\mathrm{Tr}[T^{(1)}_{f_{1}},\ldots,T^{(1)}_{f_{2n}}].

Thus the case of the Hardy space reduces to that of the weighted Bergman space.

Suppose
t>-1
, by Lemma
5.1
, the operator on
L_{a,t}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})

[T^{(t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t)}_{f_{2n}}]-[T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{2n}}]

is a trace class operator of zero trace. By Corollary
7.6
, we know that the antisymmetrization
[T^{(t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t)}_{f_{2n}}]

is itself in the trace class. Thus so does
[T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{2n}}]
. Also by Corollary
7.6
,

[T^{(t+1)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t+1)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t+1)}_{f_{2n}}]

is a trace class operator on
L_{a,t+1}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})
. Clearly

[T^{(t+1)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t+1)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t+1)}_{f_{2n}}]\big|_{L_{a,t}^{2}(\mathbb{B}_{n})}=[T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{2n}}].

Thus by Lemma
2.4
, we have the following equation

\mathrm{Tr}[T^{(t+1)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t+1)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t+1)}_{f_{2n}}]=\mathrm{Tr}[T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{2n}}].

Therefore we conclude with the following identity

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}[T^{(t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t)}_{f_{2n}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}[T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t+1,t)}_{f_{2n}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}[T^{(t+1)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t+1)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t+1)}_{f_{2n}}].

This holds for any
t>-1
. Thus by Corollary
7.6
, we have shown

\mathrm{Tr}[T^{(t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t)}_{f_{2n}}]=\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathrm{Tr}[T^{(t+k)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t+k)}_{f_{2}},\ldots,T^{(t+k)}_{f_{2n}}]=\frac{n!}{(2\pi i)^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\mathrm{d}f_{1}\wedge\mathrm{d}f_{2}\wedge\ldots\wedge\mathrm{d}f_{2n}.

This completes the proof of Theorem
8.1
.
∎

8.2
The Connes-Chern Character

As mentioned in Remark
7.5
, in this subsection, we consider the Connes-Chern character at
p>n
.

Proposition 8.2
.

Suppose
p\geq n+1
is an integer and
f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{p},g_{p}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. Then for any
t>-1
, the product
\sigma_{t}(f_{1},g_{1})\sigma_{t}(f_{2},g_{2})\ldots\sigma_{t}(f_{p},g_{p})
is in the trace class, and

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to\infty}t^{p-n}\mathrm{Tr}\bigg(\sigma_{t}(f_{1},g_{1})\sigma_{t}(f_{2},g_{2})\ldots\sigma_{t}(f_{p},g_{p})\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n^{p}}{\pi^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\prod_{j=1}^{p}C_{1}(f_{j},g_{j})(z)\frac{\mathrm{d}m(z)}{(1-|z|^{2})^{n+1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{p}}{\pi^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\partial_{i}f_{j}(z)\bar{\partial}_{i}g_{j}(z)-Rf_{j}(z)\bar{R}g_{j}(z)\bigg](1-|z|^{2})^{p-n-1}\mathrm{d}m(z).

Proof.

The proof is similar to Part 2 of Section
7.1
. Recall that the following facts were used in Part 2 and follow from Theorem
6.1
and its corollaries. For
f,g\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
, we decompose
\sigma_{t}(f,g)
as follows

\sigma_{t}(f,g)=R^{(t)}_{f,g,1}=c_{1,t}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f,g)}+R^{(t)}_{f,g,2},

(8.2)

and the following hold.

(1)

c_{1,t}=nt^{-1}+O(t^{-2})
.

(2)

C_{1}(f,g)=\phi_{1}D_{f,g,1}+\phi_{2}D_{f,g,2}
,
where

\phi_{i}(z)=(1-|z|^{2})^{i},\quad i=1,2,\ldots,

and
D_{f,g,1},D_{f,g,2}\in\mathscr{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
is defined as in (
7.21
) and (
7.22
).

(3)

R^{(t)}_{f,g,i}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},\quad i=1,2
,
and for large
t
,
\|R^{(t)}_{f,g,i}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim t^{-i+\frac{n}{p}},\quad i=1,2
.

(4)

For
t
large enough,
i,j=1,2,\ldots
, and
u,v\in\mathscr{C}^{1}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
,

(a)

T^{(t)}_{u}T^{(t)}_{v}-T^{(t)}_{uv}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},~\forall p>n,\quad\text{ and }\|T^{(t)}_{u}T^{(t)}_{v}-T^{(t)}_{uv}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{-1+\frac{n}{p}},

(b)

T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}}T^{(t)}_{u}-T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}u}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},~\forall p>\frac{n}{i+\frac{1}{2}},\quad\text{ and }\|T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}}T^{(t)}_{u}-T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}u}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{-1+\frac{n}{p}},

(c)

T^{(t)}_{u}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}}-T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}u}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},~\forall p>\frac{n}{i+\frac{1}{2}},\quad\text{ and }\|T^{(t)}_{u}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}}-T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}u}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{-1+\frac{n}{p}},

(d)

T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j}}-T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}\phi_{j}}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},~\forall p>\frac{n}{i+j},\quad\text{ and }\|T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j}}-T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}\phi_{j}}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{-1+\frac{n}{p}},

(e)

T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}u}\in\mathcal{S}^{p},~\forall p>\frac{n}{i},\quad\text{ and }\|T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}u}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim_{p}t^{\frac{n}{p}}.

Iterating Lemma
2.5
as in Remark
2.7
, we have that the property

\sigma_{t}(f_{1},g_{1})\sigma_{t}(f_{2},g_{2})\ldots\sigma_{t}(f_{p},g_{p})\in\mathcal{S}^{1}.

As in the proof of Theorem
7.3
, we write
S\sim_{a}T
when
S-T
is in trace class with trace norm converging to
0
. Then by (
8.2
), we compute

\displaystyle\sigma_{t}(f_{1},g_{1})\sigma_{t}(f_{2},g_{2})\ldots\sigma_{t}(f_{p},g_{p})-c_{1,t}^{p}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{1},g_{1})}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{2},g_{2})}\ldots T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{p},g_{p})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R^{(t)}_{f_{1},g_{1},1}R^{(t)}_{f_{2},g_{2},1}\ldots R^{(t)}_{f_{p},g_{p},1}-\big(R^{(t)}_{f_{1},g_{1},1}-R^{(t)}_{f_{1},g_{1},2}\big)\big(R^{(t)}_{f_{2},g_{2},1}-R^{(t)}_{f_{2},g_{2},2}\big)\ldots\big(R^{(t)}_{f_{p},g_{p},1}-R^{(t)}_{f_{p},g_{p},2}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum\pm R^{(t)}_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}R^{(t)}_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}\ldots R^{(t)}_{f_{p},g_{p},i_{p}},

where
i_{1},i_{2},\ldots,i_{p}\in\{1,2\}
and at least one
i_{k}=2
. Again, applying Lemma
2.5
inductively as in Remark
2.7
gives the following bounds

\|R^{(t)}_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}R^{(t)}_{f_{2},g_{2},i_{2}}\ldots R^{(t)}_{f_{p},g_{p},i_{p}}\|\leq\|R^{(t)}_{f_{1},g_{1},i_{1}}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\ldots\|R^{(t)}_{f_{p},g_{p},i_{p}}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{p}}\lesssim t^{-i_{1}+\frac{n}{p}}\cdot\ldots\cdot t^{-i_{p}+\frac{n}{p}}\leq t^{-p-1+n}.

We reach the following equation

t^{p-n}\sigma_{t}(f_{1},g_{1})\sigma_{t}(f_{2},g_{2})\ldots\sigma_{t}(f_{p},g_{p})\sim_{a}t^{p-n}c_{1,t}^{p}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{1},g_{1})}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{2},g_{2})}\ldots T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{p},g_{p})}.

(8.3)

Also, by (1) and (4)-(e), we have the following equation

t^{p-n}c_{1,t}^{p}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{1},g_{1})}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{2},g_{2})}\ldots T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{p},g_{p})}\sim_{a}n^{p}t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{1},g_{1})}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{2},g_{2})}\ldots T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{p},g_{p})}.

(8.4)

Write
u_{ij}=D_{f_{i},g_{i},j}
,
i=1,\ldots,p
,
j=1,2
. Then we arrive at the following equation,

t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{1},g_{1})}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{2},g_{2})}\ldots T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{p},g_{p})}=\sum_{j_{1},\ldots,j_{p}=1,2}t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}}u_{1j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{2}}u_{2j_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{p}}u_{pj_{p}}}.

By (4), we have the following estimate

\displaystyle t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}}u_{1j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{2}}u_{2j_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{p}}u_{pj_{p}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t^{-n}\big(T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}}u_{1j_{1}}}-T^{(t)}_{\phi_{i}}T^{(t)}_{u_{1j_{1}}}\big)T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{2}}u_{2j_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{p}}u_{pj_{p}}}+t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{1j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{2}}u_{2j_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{p}}u_{pj_{p}}}

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{1j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{2}}u_{2j_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{p}}u_{pj_{p}}}

\displaystyle\ldots

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{1j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{2}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{2j_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{p}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{pj_{p}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}}}[T^{(t)}_{u_{1j_{1}}},T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{2}}}]T^{(t)}_{u_{2j_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{p}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{pj_{p}}}+t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{2}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{1j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{2j_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{p}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{pj_{p}}}

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{2}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{1j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{2j_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{p}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{pj_{p}}}

\displaystyle\ldots

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{p}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{1j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{2j_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{u_{pj_{p}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t^{-n}\big(T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{2}}}-T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}+j_{2}}}\big)T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{3}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{p}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{1j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{2j_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{u_{pj_{p}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}+j_{2}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{3}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{p}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{1j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{2j_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{u_{pj_{p}}}

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}+j_{2}}}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{3}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{p}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{1j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{2j_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{u_{pj_{p}}}

\displaystyle\ldots

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}+j_{2}+\ldots+j_{p}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{1j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{2j_{2}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{u_{pj_{p}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}+j_{2}+\ldots+j_{p}}}\big(T^{(t)}_{u_{1j_{1}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{2j_{2}}}-T^{(t)}_{u_{1j_{1}}u_{2j_{2}}}\big)T^{(t)}_{u_{3j_{3}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{u_{pj_{p}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}+j_{2}+\ldots+j_{p}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{1j_{1}}u_{2j_{2}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{3j_{3}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{u_{pj_{p}}}

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}+j_{2}+\ldots+j_{p}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{1j_{1}}u_{2j_{2}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{3j_{3}}}\ldots T^{(t)}_{u_{pj_{p}}}

\displaystyle\ldots

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}+j_{2}+\ldots+j_{p}}}T^{(t)}_{u_{1j_{1}}u_{2j_{2}}\ldots u_{pj_{p}}}

\displaystyle\sim_{a}

\displaystyle t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{\phi_{j_{1}+j_{2}+\ldots+j_{p}}u_{1j_{1}}u_{2j_{2}}\ldots u_{pj_{p}}}.

Adding up over
j_{1},j_{2},\ldots,j_{p}=1,2
, we get the following equation

t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{1},g_{1})}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{2},g_{2})}\ldots T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{p},g_{p})}\sim_{a}t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{1},g_{1})C_{1}(f_{2},g_{2})\ldots C_{1}(f_{p},g_{p})}.

(8.5)

Combining (
8.3
) (
8.4
) and (
8.5
), we arrive at the following equation

t^{p-n}\sigma_{t}(f_{1},g_{1})\sigma_{t}(f_{2},g_{2})\ldots\sigma_{t}(f_{p},g_{p})\sim_{a}n^{p}t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{C_{1}(f_{1},g_{1})C_{1}(f_{2},g_{2})\ldots C_{1}(f_{p},g_{p})}.

(8.6)

Denote

F=C_{1}(f_{1},g_{1})C_{1}(f_{2},g_{2})\ldots C_{1}(f_{p},g_{p}).

Then
|F(z)|\lesssim(1-|z|^{2})^{p}
.
By
[
47
, Lemma 2.5]
and
2.3
, we compute
\mathrm{Tr}\big(n^{p}t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{F}\big)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\big(n^{p}t^{-n}T^{(t)}_{F}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{p}t^{-n}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\langle T^{(t)}_{F}K^{(t)}_{\xi},K^{(t)}_{\xi}\rangle\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(\xi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{p}t^{-n}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}F(z)K^{(t)}_{\xi}(z)K^{(t)}_{z}(\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(\xi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{p}t^{-n}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}F(z)K^{(t)}_{\xi}(z)K^{(t)}_{z}(\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(\xi)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{p}t^{-n}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}F(z)K^{(t)}_{z}(z)\mathrm{d}\lambda_{t}(z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{p}t^{-n}\frac{(n-1)!}{\pi^{n}B(n,t+1)}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\frac{F(z)}{(1-|z|^{2})^{n+1}}\mathrm{d}m(z)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\frac{n^{p}}{\pi^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\frac{F(z)}{(1-|z|^{2})^{n+1}}\mathrm{d}m(z),\quad t\to\infty.

This gives the first equation. The second equation follows from plugging in the formula of
C_{1}(f,g)
in Remark
6.5
.
This completes the proof of Proposition
8.2
.
∎

Recall that
C_{1}(f,g)-C_{1}(g,f)=\frac{-i}{n}\{f,g\}
. Thus Proposition
8.2
implies the following.

Corollary 8.3
.

Suppose
p\geq n+1
is an integer and
f_{1},g_{1},\ldots,f_{p},g_{p}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. Then for any
t\geq-1
,
[T^{(t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t)}_{g_{1}}][T^{(t)}_{f_{2}},T^{(t)}_{g_{2}}]\ldots[T^{(t)}_{f_{p}},T^{(t)}_{g_{p}}]
is in the trace class, and

\lim_{t\to\infty}t^{p-n}\mathrm{Tr}\bigg([T^{(t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t)}_{g_{1}}][T^{(t)}_{f_{2}},T^{(t)}_{g_{2}}]\ldots[T^{(t)}_{f_{p}},T^{(t)}_{g_{p}}]\bigg)=\frac{(-i)^{p}}{\pi^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\{f_{j},g_{j}\}(z)\frac{\mathrm{d}m(z)}{(1-|z|^{2})^{n+1}}.

We can compare the formula above with the following Dixmier trace formula obtained by Engliš, Guo and Zhang in
[
22
]
.

Tr_{\omega}[T^{(t)}_{f_{1}},T^{(t)}_{g_{1}}]\ldots[T^{(t)}_{f_{n}},T^{(t)}_{g_{n}}]=\frac{1}{n!}\int_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\prod_{j=1}^{n}\{f_{j},g_{j}\}(z)\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma(z)}{\sigma_{2n-1}}.

Also recall the identity

\sigma_{t}(f,g)=-H^{(t)*}_{\bar{f}}H^{(t)}_{g}.

Thus taking
f_{i}=\bar{g},g_{i}=g,i=1,\ldots,p
in Proposition
8.2
gives the following asymptotic formula for Schatten-norm of Hankel operators.

Corollary 8.4
.

Suppose
p\geq n+1
is an integer, and
g\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. Then

\lim_{t\to\infty}t^{p-n}\|H^{(t)}_{g}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{2p}}^{2p}=\frac{1}{\pi^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\bigg[|\bar{\partial}g(z)|^{2}-|\bar{R}g(z)|^{2}\bigg]^{p}(1-|z|^{2})^{p-n-1}\mathrm{d}m(z).

Remark 8.5
.

There are profound study of Schatten-class membership and Schatten norm formulas for Hankel operators. See
[
3
,
28
,
29
,
41
,
51
]
for Schatten-class membership criteria of Hankel operators. For
q=2,4,6
, Janson, Upmeier and Wallstén
[
34
]
gave the following identity on the Hardy space of the unit disk.

\|H_{\phi}\|_{\mathcal{S}^{q}}^{q}=c_{q}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{|\psi(\zeta)-\psi(\tau)|^{q}}{|\zeta-\tau|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\sigma(\zeta)\mathrm{d}\sigma(\tau),

where
c_{q}
are constants, and
\psi=(I-P)\phi
. In fact, it was shown that such identities hold only for
q=2,4,6
. Recently, Xia
[
50
]
extended this formula to the open unit ball
\mathbb{B}_{n}
.

Finally, Proposition
8.2
gives the following asymptotic formula for the Connes-Chern character at
p>n
.

Theorem 8.6
.

Suppose
p\geq n+1
is an integer and
f_{0},f_{1},\ldots,f_{2p-1}\in\mathscr{C}^{2}(\overline{\mathbb{B}_{n}})
. Set
f_{2p}:=f_{0}
. Then

\begin{split}&\lim_{t\to\infty}t^{p-n}\tau_{t}(f_{0},f_{1},\ldots,f_{2p-1})\\
=&\frac{n^{p}}{\pi^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{B}_{n}}\bigg(\prod_{j=0}^{p-1}C_{1}(f_{2j},f_{2j+1})(z)-\prod_{j=0}^{p-1}C_{1}(f_{2j+1},f_{2j+2})(z)\bigg)\frac{\mathrm{d}m(z)}{(1-|z|^{2})^{n+1}}.\end{split}
